[[smu-dv-verification-plan]]
== SMU OCAH Open-Source Verification Plan

=== Overview

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Field |Value
|Design Under Test |`hw/top/smu_wrapper.sv` through `hw/sys/smu/dv/tb/tb_wrapper_top.sv`
|Framework |PyUVM on cocotb; an SV-UVM view (`--framework uvm`, VCS) for the five scenarios that carry one
|Primary simulator |Verilator
|Reference testbench architecture |`SMU_TB_ARCH.adoc`
|Executable contract |the enrolled groups in `testlists/all.toml` (`--dut smu`)
|===

This plan describes what the SMU open-source suite verifies and against which
RTL. The executable contract is the enrolled testlist groups; this document
describes them, it does not replace them.

Five scenarios carry a second implementation in SystemVerilog UVM under the
same name (`module = { cocotb = "<name>", uvm = "<name>" }` in
`testlists/all.toml`; `hw/sys/smu/dv/README.md`, "SystemVerilog UVM
framework"). The enrolled groups run the cocotb view; every `result.json` a
regression writes under `build/runs/` records `framework: cocotb`. The
checker rows marked `SV-UVM shape only` below describe the SV-UVM view of a
card and close nothing for the cocotb regression's sign-off; a card's cocotb
checkers alone carry its Pass Criteria there. Under `--framework uvm` the
SV-UVM rows are the governing set of that card instead, and the card's
negative plusarg must fail the run.

=== Verification Strategy

The SMU is verified at its own boundary on the production wrapper
(`hw/top/smu_wrapper.sv`, one compile profile, `compile_smu_chiplet`): the
SMC, DTP and SEP below it are real RTL, the SEP runs its EL2 core, and the
external SMN fabric is driven by the shared AXI VIP. Four surfaces are covered
on the wrapper, plus the SEP firmware set:

. *Fabric and address decode* — external SMN AXI into SMC, ID-width conversion,
  crossbar error handling, ATOP rejection, alias remap and the inbound and
  outbound filters, driven both from the external port and through JTAG2AXI.
. *SMC under SMU* — bring-up, reset control, mailbox, watchdog, GPIO straps and
  the security demote path, reached through the SMU rather than at the SMC
  boundary.
. *DTP under SMU* — primary JTAG, STAP selection, boundary-scan and iJTAG, the
  OTP bridge and the cross-trigger routing, as they appear once DTP sits under
  the SMU.
. *Clock, reset and boot sequencing* — clock-stop coordination, boot stall,
  IC_RESET domains and external boot-sequence gating.
. *SEP firmware under SMU* — SEP boot and firmware execution, lifecycle state
  broadcast from the SEP eFuse shadow to the SMC and the DTP, and the entropy
  stack, with the verdict taken from the firmware's own terminal loop.

Checking is centralised in `SmuScoreboard`, which refuses a vacuous PASS and
requires every mapped evidence token to have been proven by a real check. See
the checking strategy in `SMU_TB_ARCH.adoc`.

=== Naming Rules

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Rule |Requirement
|Single-scenario module |Use the module name, for example `smu_clock_stop_coordination_test`
|Multi-scenario module |Use `+smu_<feature>_<scenario>_test+`, for example
`smu_axi_filter_in_instance_matrix_test`
|Scenario names |Use descriptive lower-snake-case names that encode operation,
mode, direction or coverage target
|Names from another subsystem |Normalise to `+smu_*+` and keep the target inside
the name, for example `smu_smc_wdt_sanity_test` for the SMC watchdog reached
through the SMU
|Open-source files |One PyUVM test class per scenario under
`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`, so each scenario is independently selectable from
a testlist. Sequences live in `cocotb/seq_lib/` (those written against the
SMU's own interfaces) and `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/` (those written against
the wrapper's), two halves of one namespace package, where scenarios differ
only in stimulus. The SV-UVM view keeps the same basenames: one test class per
scenario under `uvm/tests/`, its scenario sequence under `uvm/seq_lib/`
|===

=== BFM Policy

The BFM table lives in `SMU_TB_ARCH.adoc` so the testbench chapter and the plan
cannot drift apart. The policy itself: standard protocols use the shared OCAH
VIP packages, SMU-specific behaviour uses OCAH-local models under `env/`, and
no enrolled test may reach into the DUT with Force or deposit.

=== Verification Scope

This plan claims SMU behaviour at the `smu_wrapper` boundary, with the DUT
elaborated on the `compile_smu_chiplet` profile (`SEP=1`, the real SEP core,
the open-source IP integration attached). The behaviours below sit inside a block another
plan owns, behind a pad the bench does not model, or on a path the tree has
no stimulus for; a green SMU regression says nothing about them, and
`hw/sys/smu/doc/dv/SMU_DEFERRED_DISPOSITION.adoc` records the disposition of
every name that was catalogued against them.

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Behaviour |Why it is out of scope here
|The SMC's internal behaviour (CSRs, peripherals, firmware boot beyond the smoke images) |The SMC plan, `hw/sys/smc/dv/docs/SMC_VPLAN.adoc`; this plan proves what crosses the SMU boundary and the SMC-under-SMU paths
|The SEP's internal behaviour, its crypto blocks and its lifecycle-controller policy |The SEP plan, `hw/sys/sep/dv/docs/SEP_VPLAN.adoc`; this plan observes the SEP through the wrapper's boot, lifecycle and JTAG-gating outputs
|The DTP's block-level behaviour (TAP controller, JTAG2AXI bridge internals, iJTAG instruments) |The DTP plan, `hw/sys/dtp/dv/docs/DTP_VPLAN.adoc`; this plan proves the DTP-under-SMU paths the wrapper exposes
|Boundary-scan cells, adopter DFD and DFT scan chains |No cells in the wrapper; the bench loops every host scan chain back on itself (an approved stand-in)
|The eFuse macros |`efuse_bank_model` answers every sense behind the shim (an approved stand-in)
|The asynchronous cold-reset assertion path |The RTL de-glitches the assertion; the scenario is catalogued in the disposition record with the RTL ruling it waits on
|Live SMC↔SEP mailbox challenge-response and SEP=1 fetch/retire as feature credit |Firmware-driven SEP scenarios need the toolchain container; they run in `all`, not in the scheduled `hosted` set, and count as observe-only
|The SEP=0 composition, `smu #(.SEP(0))`: the direct ID-width converters in place of `smu_axi_xbar`, the `lc_state` and demote tie-offs, the SEP=0 OTP error slave |Not verified in this tree: no target of this package elaborates SEP=0. The names that prove that shape are catalogued `not-runnable` in `hw/sys/smu/doc/dv/SMU_DEFERRED_DISPOSITION.adoc`, bodies in git history, and that record owns them until a plan builds the elaboration they need
|===

[[smu-scope-traceability]]
=== Scope and Traceability

This section is the requirement-to-test-to-coverage matrix for features
F1–F7, which the <<smu-feature-list,hosted feature list>> under Signoff
Package defines together with the hosted subset and the per-leaf
`TEST_EVIDENCE` tokens; `hw/sys/smu/doc/dv/SMU_DEFERRED_DISPOSITION.adoc`
classifies the OUT names. Live group
membership for `--dut smu` is `testlists/all.toml`.

[cols=",",options="header",]
|===
|Field |Value
|DUT |`--dut smu` → `hw/top/smu_wrapper.sv` under `smu_wrapper_uvm_top`, on its compile profile `compile_smu_chiplet` (alias `smu_wrapper`)
|Spec / plan |<<smu-feature-list,hosted feature list>> Features 1–7
|Coverage intent |`TEST_EVIDENCE` tokens for feature credit; the `cov/sv` cover properties (the `user` family) on Verilator and VCS, and toggle over the SMU's own logic on VCS (`hw/sys/smu/doc/dv/SMU_COVERAGE_POLICY.adoc`)
|Checker |`SmuScoreboard` named `evidence=` / `expect_*` tokens + `results.xml`
|CI |`.github/workflows/sim.yml` (PR gate) runs `smoke`; `.github/workflows/regress.yml` runs `hosted`
|===

[[smu-live-groups]]
==== Live groups versus credit

Executable membership, `--dut smu` only:

[cols="2,3",options="header",]
|===
|Group |Role
|`smoke` |PR gate (`sim.yml`). Toolchain-free; `fw_boot` reaches the SEP firmware and cannot run on a hosted runner.
|`hosted` |`regress.yml` regression. Feature-credit set.
|`all` |Adds the SEP=1 firmware set. Not the `regress.yml` denominator.
|===

`hosted` and the SEP=1 groups are two evidence surfaces on one DUT:
`--dut smu` is the wrapper (`testlists/all.toml`), and `regress.yml`
runs its `hosted`. PASSes carry `DUT_TAG=WRAPPER`.

[[smu-credit-rules]]
==== Credit rules

* Feature credit requires a named `TEST_EVIDENCE` token the sequence
  already emits and a non-vacuous `SmuScoreboard` count. The map must not
  invent a checkbox the body cannot prove.
* Credit is the enrolled `hosted` set.
* Every name in `hw/sys/smu/doc/dv/SMU_DEFERRED_DISPOSITION.adoc` is OUT and reports no PASS.
* The SEP=0 composition is OUT of this matrix: no target elaborates it, and
  its names are `not-runnable` in the disposition record.
* SEP=1 instantiate / fetch / retire (F2) and live SMC↔SEP mailbox
  challenge-response (F5) are OUT.
* Wrapper elaboration / reset is `smu_wrapper_elaboration_test`
  (wrapper `fw_boot` and `elaboration`).

`smu_sep_smoke_test` on `--dut smu` is F1 observe / F6 export on the
wrapper. It is not F2 credit.

=== Scenario-Expanded Test Summary

Every card in <<smu-test-procedures>>, one row each, in card order: the
members of the wrapper `all` group (`testlists/all.toml`, `--dut smu`). The
Leaf column is the family section the card sits in (`wrapper` is the
wrapper-native SEP firmware and probe set); the Scenario column is the
one-line intent.

[cols="2,1,3",options="header",]
|===
|Test |Leaf |Scenario
|`smu_axi_alias_remap_manager_scope_test` |fabric |SMU Tier A alias-remap (S3 LIVE)
|`smu_axi_atomic_operation_test` |fabric |non-ATOP SMN read completes (RID==ARID); ATOP not drivable, TB ties aw.atop low
|`smu_axi_crossbar_error_handling_test` |fabric |SMU_ALL_008 Option-B PWRGOOD-only
|`smu_axi_filter_allow_ns_test` |fabric |SMU Tier A allow_ns (SEP=1 J2A + s_axi)
|`smu_axi_filter_in_instance_matrix_test` |fabric |SMU Tier A inbound instance matrix
|`smu_axi_filter_out_instance_matrix_test` |fabric |SMU Tier A outbound instance DECODE
|`smu_axi_id_width_conversion_test` |fabric |An 8-bit-ID `ext_in` read reaches an SMC register through the SEP=1 crossbar and returns OKAY with RID == ARID.
|`smu_axi_prot_encoding_decode_test` |fabric |SMU Tier A AxPROT S9 (SEP=1 J2A + s_axi)
|`smu_fabric_reg_bar_wr_test` |fabric |SMU Tier A fabric config CSR delivery
|`smu_otp_vs_fabric_map_race_test` |fabric |OTP vs fabric MAP last-writer
|`smu_sys_in_filter_program_jtag_test` |fabric |inbound0 program then SMN OKAY
|`smu_sys_in_filter_reprogram_shrink_test` |fabric |wide, shrink, clear
|`smu_sys_in_filter_window_edge_test` |fabric |page interior OKAY; adjacent page each side DECERR
|`smu_xtrig_ctm_remap_test` |fabric |product-pin CTM remap
|`smu_axi_in_burst_outstanding_test` |fabric |The inbound SMN port carries several transfers in flight and refuses the burst types the register targets do not support.
|`smu_axi_in_attribute_sweep_test` |fabric |Every AW/AR attribute the inbound SMN port carries is answered as the traffic-filter specification assigns, and one SMC write leaves the chiplet on `smu_axi_out`.
|`smu_axi_in_sep_aperture_test` |fabric |Inbound SMN traffic into the SEP aperture: SEP SRAM round trips under every id, qualifier, size, offset and burst swept, the SEP error responses, and every aperture address bit
|`smu_smc_inbound_window_sweep_test` |fabric |Inbound SMN traffic into the SMC eFuse shim, external target and DTP control window under every AxPROT, the external target at every size and low address bit, and SMC SPM writes under distinct IDs held in flight.
|`smu_axi_out_addr_len_size_test` |fabric |What the SMC masters a bench can drive send out of the chiplet crosses smu_axi_out with the address, AxSIZE and AxLEN they were given.
|`smu_aperture_map_walk_test` |fabric |The SMU crossbar decodes the SMC aperture at every base and size BASE_CONFIG admits and JTAG2AXI can follow.
|`smu_xbar_connectivity_matrix_test` |fabric |An `ext_in` access that matches neither programmed aperture is answered DECERR by the crossbar and never reaches `ext_out`.
|`smu_periph_ext_window_test` |fabric |The SMC peripheral crossbar's macro AXI-Lite window is reachable over JTAG2AXI, decodes its sub-windows, and shrinks with the SMC aperture.
|`smu_boot_stall_jtag_cold_reset_matrix_test` |reset |boot-stall sticky matrix
|`smu_boot_stall_vs_ic_reset_priority_test` |reset |stall vs IC_RESET priority
|`smu_dft_dtp_boot_stall_test` |reset |DTP DEBUG_CONTROL boot-stall -> SMC fuse_reset
|`smu_dft_gpio_boot_stall_test` |reset |GPIO pad boot-stall gates fuse_reset
|`smu_ext_boot_seq_gate_test` |reset |SMU P0 external boot-sequence gate (SMU_006)
|`smu_ic_reset_dual_domain_illegal_test` |reset |dual IC_RESET in one DR
|`smu_ic_reset_smc_multi_domain_test` |reset |IC_RESET SMC multi-domain
|`smu_ic_reset_ss_domain_matrix_test` |reset |IC_RESET SS cold/warm domains
|`smu_jtag_reset_override_test` |reset |IC_RESET TDR override of EXT/SMC slices
|`smu_dtp_ptap_ir_walk_test` |dtp |Every PTAP IR encoding decodes to its own bit, and the Pause and Exit2 TAP states reach the boundary
|`smu_dtp_jtag2axi_address_walk_test` |dtp |Every address bit of the SMC fabric and both OTP JTAG2AXI bridges, and of the adopter and DTP CSR windows behind the SMC, reaches its target
|`smu_smc_reset_ctrl_test` |reset |primary/cold/periph reset release under the SMU wrapper
|`smu_reset_release_sync_test` |reset |Cold and primary reset deassertions land on a clock edge of the domain each output is synchronised to.
|`smu_sram_auto_init_done_test` |reset |With SRAM auto-initialisation enabled, a cold reset clears `smc_init_mem_done_o`, the zeroing sweep runs, and the output asserts and holds when it completes.
|`smu_sram_auto_init_disabled_test` |reset |With SRAM auto-initialisation held off, no zeroing sweep starts and no zeroing write reaches the scratch RAM, and `smc_init_mem_done_o` reads low while the cold reset is asserted; what it does otherwise is recorded, not claimed.
|`smu_clock_domain_composition_test` |reset |The telemetry, SEP-watchdog and peripheral clock and reset domains reach their consumers as their own domains and stay independent of `clk_smu_i`.
|`smu_smc_ss_reset_ctrl_sweep_test` |reset |The seven SMC reset-unit registers that own the fields of `ss_reset_ctrl_o` drive the 32 subsystem elements lane for lane, every field bit is driven both ways, and a `SS_COLD_RESET_LOCK` bit blocks its own cold-reset lane while the rest of the write lands.
|`smu_clock_stop_coordination_test` |smu |SMU_ALL_006 boot-stall/IC-reset/clkstop
|`smu_i3c_mem_port_connectivity_test` |smu |FAB_SMC_031 local peripheral delivery
|`smu_jtag2axi_vs_smn_same_csr_race_test` |smu |J2A vs SMN coherent winner
|`smu_system_timer_octs_test` |smu |SMU Tier C OCTS primary free-run
|`smu_xtrig_ctm_illegal_phase_test` |smu |pin-only illegal CTM corners
|`smu_boundary_port_composition_test` |smu |The SEP=1 SMU boundary ports are present at the widths the specifications state, the SMC-side ID-width converter presents the 6-bit subsystem ID the SMC system AXI input specifies, `ss_config_o` and `skip_mem_repair_o` present their specified idle values, and the CTP channels stay inert while their data inputs rest.
|`smu_composition_parameter_test` |smu |The build parameters the wrapper passes down reach the instances that consume them at the values the specification states.
|`smu_smc_boundary_io_test` |smu |The SMC-facing wrapper boundary signals that `port_table.adoc` declares are proved through the SMC register map or, for the external interrupts, the SMC CPU interrupt vector `interrupts.adoc` defines, each leg comparing a DUT-produced value against the contract.
|`smu_smc_cool_reset_pin_test` |smu |A 28-`clk_ref` pulse on `rst_cool_n_from_pin_i` is filtered and a hold past that pulse asserts the SMC primary reset; the four DFT done/pass straps are paired with their set-only DFX_CTRL.STATUS_SMU fields through that reset.
|`smu_smc_gpio_pad_output_test` |smu |Every GPIO pad driven from its own DATA_CTRL, with a code no other pad shares, follows CORE2PAD and loops it back to its own interrupt lane, and transmit alone drives the pad with its interrupt lane low
|`smu_smc_boundary_lane_sweep_test` |smu |Every lane of the multi-lane SMC-facing wrapper buses is driven both ways and compared against the SMC register or interrupt vector that owns it, and each aggregate names the lane count it expects.
|`smu_smc_efuse_shadow_sense_reset_test` |smu |After a real SMC fuse sense, `smc_shadow_regs_o` carries the sensed eFuse image at every register offset `smc_addr.h` gives the map, and it reads the zero every `smc_efuse_map.rdl` field resets to while `rst_cold_ni` is held; the image programs every fuse bit, so both samples are taken on the full width of the port.
|`smu_telemetry_atb_capture_test` |smu |One complete ATB telemetry message entered at the wrapper pins is captured by each receiver and read back, the ATB flush handshake completes at the pins, and the port applies backpressure when its receiver stops.
|`smu_smc_peripheral_irq_test` |smu |The raw GPIO and UART interrupt vectors `port_table.adoc` brings out of the wrapper follow their sources both ways.
|`smu_cla_action_test` |smu |The SMC CLA node 0 custom actions, cross-trigger outputs and clock-halt report, programmed over JTAG2AXI, reach the SMU nets they drive and clear again.
|`smu_dtp_bsr_extest_loopback_test` |dtp |EXTEST BSR scan loopback
|`smu_dtp_bsr_ijtag_scan_test` |dtp |BSR .select gating (EXTEST / SAMPLE_PRELOAD)
|`smu_dtp_io_stap_smoke_test` |dtp |DTP-IO-STAP host TCK observe
|`smu_dtp_jtag2axi_back_to_back_error_ok_test` |dtp |DECERR then immediate OK
|`smu_dtp_jtag2axi_smc_error_path_test` |dtp |SMC J2A unmapped DECERR
|`smu_dtp_jtag2axi_smc_rw_matrix_test` |dtp |WSTRB-width + partial merge on SPM
|`smu_dtp_jtag2axi_wstrb_partial_sticky_test` |dtp |partial WSTRB + neighbor
|`smu_dtp_jtag_smc_cpu_register_test` |dtp |CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_15 via J2A
|`smu_dtp_jtag_smoke_test` |dtp |SMU_ALL_005 PTAP IDCODE/BYPASS/TRST
|`smu_dtp_otp_smc_complete_rw_test` |dtp |OTP MAP RESERVED walk (SEP=1, no Force)
|`smu_dtp_otp_smc_map_rw_test` |dtp |SMC OTP J2A MAP SPARE[0] R/W (SEP=1, no Force)
|`smu_dtp_otp_smc_series_error_test` |dtp |OTP series NO_INCR + decode-hole SLVERR
|`smu_dtp_smc_stap_smoke_test` |dtp |DTP-SMC-STAP TRST + TAP_3DCR select
|`smu_dtp_sep_dm_dmi_test` |dtp |DTP TAP → SEP debug-module `dmstatus` over the SEP TAP's `dmi` register
|`smu_dtp_sep_dm_sba_test` |dtp |DTP TAP → SEP debug-module system-bus writes of the SEP aperture base and DEMOTE_2, and a SEP read and write through the crossbar, observed at the SMU boundary
|`smu_sep_sba_fabric_sweep_test` |dtp |SEP debug system bus over every path that leaves the SEP: the SMU aperture, the alias and output remaps, responder errors, the SMC window and the external and TRNG apertures
|`smu_sep_lsu_fabric_test` |dtp |The SEP load/store unit, run by the debug module from an ICCM probe, against the SMU aperture under responder backpressure, the SMC SPM and the external aperture
|`smu_efuse_command_test` |dtp |SEP and SMC eFuse read and program commands on the fuse-command ports, driven from the SEP debug system bus
|`smu_sep_sba_peripheral_test` |dtp |The SEP mailbox interrupts, SPI host quad lanes and interrupt, security-disable token and watchdog reset request, driven from the SEP debug system bus and compared at the SMU.
|`smu_jtag_chain_enhanced_test` |dtp |IDCODE/BYPASS at 1/5/10/20 MHz TCK
|`smu_smc_dtp_jtag2axi_smoke_test` |dtp |SMC fabric J2A smoke (SEP=1, no Force)
|`smu_dtp_scan_chain_boundary_test` |dtp |The iJTAG SIB hosts and the secondary TAPs leave and re-enter `smu_wrapper` at the pads, and the shift path runs through those boundary pins.
|`smu_otp_bridges_under_dbg_disable_test` |dtp |The SMC and SEP OTP JTAG2AXI bridges complete while the lifecycle posture has closed the SMC fabric bridge.
|`smu_otp_prod_error_resp_test` |dtp |Under PROD the SMC and SEP eFuse JTAG policies refuse OTP bridge accesses with DECERR, and the accesses PROD keeps complete.
|`smu_xtrig_ctp_pad_test` |dtp |The four CTP pad groups of `smu_wrapper` follow the cross-trigger network's mode, handshake and matrix routing at the pads, and a wire-OR port receives cycle-exactly on its shared wire's assertion edge.
|`smu_xtrig_mode_composition_test` |dtp |The per-internal-CT mode vector reaches the DTP, and a handshake lane acknowledges where a pulse-sync lane does not.
|`smu_sep_smoke_test` |sep |wrapper SEP RTL boot: ROM fetch, ICCM, 256 retires / 16 PCs, DCCM write
|`smu_sep_fuse_sense_done_test` |sep |SEP fuse sense completes on the composition and reports on `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` after the cold reset released and after eFuse read traffic appeared.
|`smu_sep_secure_tm_test` |sep |`secure_tm_req_i` is latched into `secure_tm_o` once per cold reset from the value it holds at the SEP fuse-sense-done edge, not the value it held when the reset asserted, and the strap is ignored between sampling windows.
|`smu_smc_mailbox_int_test` |smc |smc_ext_mailbox_interrupts width plus mailbox 0 / 31 bit mapping (SMU_ALL_004)
|`smu_smc_mailbox_sanity_test` |smc |outbound-0 STATUS/IRQEN via fabric J2A
|`smu_smc_smoke_test` |smc |wrapper SMC ROM boot to PASS; ROM-read and scratch-write floors (no_sep)
|`smu_smc_fabric_test` |smc |SMC ROM firmware on all four harts stores to and reads back SEP SRAM over `smc_out` and an `ext_out` address, with the bench cross-checking every word
|`smu_smc_wdt_sanity_test` |smc |CORE0 WDT unlock + CMP via J2A
|`smu_smc_wdt_timeout_irq_test` |smc |CORE0 WDT sticky WDOGIP0
|`smu_smc_fuse_sense_sequence_test` |smc |SMC fuse sense completes once after the primary reset released and after eFuse read traffic appeared, and the delayed fuse reset releases after that rise rather than with the cold reset.
|`smu_smc_rom_boot_min_pass_test` |smc |The SMC CPU fetches and executes the checked-in ROM stub on the wrapper and lands its magic word in CPU_CTRL scratch0, visible to an external master.
|`smu_smc_wdt_boundary_timeout_test` |smc |The CORE0 watchdog's first and second timeouts reach the SMU pins and the second one drops the SMC warm reset.
|`smu_wrapper_elaboration_test` |wrapper |SMU_ALL_001: the SEP=1 wrapper composes SMC+SEP+DTP+xbar on one clock/reset domain and exports the SEP lifecycle state.
|`smu_wrapper_jtag_ptap_test` |wrapper |The primary TAP answers on the production wrapper while the SEP is executing, and TAP access does not disturb it.
|`smu_sep_boot_health_test` |wrapper |The SEP boots the real DV boot-health image and parks in its own pass loop, with the boot order observed.
|`smu_sep_rom_tcm_load_test` |wrapper |The SEP loads its own ICCM from boot ROM and executes it, with no testbench TCM preload.
|`smu_sep_sanity_test` |wrapper |The SEP runs SHA-256 (HMAC) and SHA3-256 (KMAC) known-answer vectors on-chip and reports PASS through the mailbox.
|`smu_sep_modules_test` |wrapper |The SEP module matrix -- AES, HMAC and KMAC in this build -- clears on-chip, with the failing module named.
|`smu_sep_aes_test` |wrapper |One AES-128 ECB known-answer vector is encrypted and compared on-chip, with the alert status checked before the image parks.
|`smu_sep_otbn_test` |wrapper |The OTBN register aperture is reachable over the SEP-internal fabric -- a reachability claim, not an OTBN functional one.
|`smu_sep_entropy_test` |wrapper |The SEP firmware brings up ESRC -> CSRNG -> EDN and entropy is proven to flow, not merely to have been programmed.
|`smu_sep_efuse_test` |wrapper |The SEP-side eFuse control and external-shim CSR read/write path clears on-chip.
|`smu_sep_wdt_test` |wrapper |The SEP watchdog control and threshold register path clears on-chip.
|`smu_sep_dma_test` |wrapper |The SEP DMA engine register path clears on-chip.
|`smu_sep_spi_test` |wrapper |The SEP OpenTitan SPI host request reaches the SMC SPI pins as a DV-owned, spec-cited pin table states, on every cycle, with the firmware's SCK count, CS# framing, output enables and transmit bytes at the pads, a seeded receive word returned from the pad to RXDATA, and no pad change while the host is idle.
|`smu_sep_remap_test` |wrapper |The AP and STEE output-remap region-0 offsets end up at the goldens the firmware names.
|`smu_sep_smc_notify_test` |wrapper |The SEP outbound egress path is proven segment by segment, not inferred from the arriving magic.
|`smu_sep_bidirect_test` |wrapper |SEP and SMC complete a bidirectional handshake across the SMU crossbar, with both directions load-bearing.
|`smu_sep_lcc_flow_test` |wrapper |The SEP lifecycle posture is earned by the SEP eFuse sense and then driven by firmware, and both movements are traced to every consumer as a delta off the pre-sense posture.
|`smu_sep_lcc_state_test_dev_test` |wrapper |Lifecycle decode for this state: `lc_state` must reach `0xf0` at the SMC, with `dbg_disable` clear at the DTP.
|`smu_sep_lcc_state_prod_test` |wrapper |Lifecycle decode for this state: `lc_state` must reach `0xe1` at the SMC, with `dbg_disable` clear at the DTP.
|`smu_sep_lcc_state_prod_end_test` |wrapper |Lifecycle decode for this state: `lc_state` must reach `0x78` at the SMC, with `dbg_disable` asserted at the DTP.
|`smu_sep_lcc_state_rma_chiplet_test` |wrapper |Lifecycle decode for this state: `lc_state` must reach `0x96` at the SMC, with `dbg_disable` clear at the DTP.
|`smu_sep_dbg_gating_test_dev_test` |wrapper |Debug is open in this state: the JTAG2AXI bridge must launch the transaction on the debug AXI port.
|`smu_sep_dbg_gating_prod_end_test` |wrapper |Debug is gated in this state: the JTAG2AXI bridge must not launch the transaction at all.
|`smu_dtp_sep_smc_chain_prod_test` |wrapper |SEP decides, DTP enforces, SMC serves: the demote opens debug and the JTAG2AXI read returns the SMC's live scratch value.
|`smu_dtp_sep_smc_chain_prod_end_test` |wrapper |The same firmware and the same JTAG operation under an eFuse image that ignores the demote: debug stays disabled and the read must not reach AXI.
|===

[[smu-test-procedures]]
=== Test Procedures

Each entry below is a test body and its sequence: one card for every member
of the wrapper `all` group (`testlists/all.toml`, `--dut smu`), and no card for
anything else. Scenarios
that cannot run or cannot pass on this bench are recorded in
`hw/sys/smu/doc/dv/SMU_DEFERRED_DISPOSITION.adoc`, not here. The
Checkers table lists the evidence tokens the body emits, and those tokens are
also the `(CHK_ID, TOKEN, EXPECT)` rows in `cocotb/env/smu_evidence_map.py`.
Those rows are a token contract, not a behavioural one: the gate that
applies them, `prove_mapped_features()` in the base test, what
it fails on, which leaves it skips and what a satisfied row does and does not
establish are in `SMU_TB_ARCH.adoc` ("Checking Strategy"). To know what a
row's EXPECT sentence is worth, read the check.

==== FABRIC

[[sec-001-smu_axi_alias_remap_manager_scope_test]]
===== 1 `smu_axi_alias_remap_manager_scope_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_axi_alias_remap_manager_scope_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`

*Objective*: FAB_SMC_018 S3 J2A+SPM; S1/S2/S4/S5 not covered; no Force.

*Test Coverage*:

* OSS SMU Tier A: alias-remap manager scope (FAB_SMC_018 subset).
* SEP=1 honest scope (no sep_in / no Force): S3 J2A programs alias region[0], writes via alias window, proves remapped SPM consumer readback (jtag manager path through smc_alias_remap_wrap).
* S1 DEFERRED-NOTE — DMA data_accel observe needs DMA bring-up.
* S2 DEFERRED-NOTE — Log Engine observe needs log_engine stimulus.
* S4/S5 DEFERRED-NOTE — commercial SF-245 / SF-252 (unchanged).
* Commercial hierarchical AW watch on axi_to_input_mux_req.* replaced by SPM consumer proof at the remapped target (same address math).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Drive `smu_axi_alias_remap_manager_scope_test_seq` over the wrapper's product interfaces.
. Compare every access against its stated expectation in the scoreboard.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-ALIAS-REMAP-J2A-READY` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-ALIAS-REMAP-SCOPE-S3` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `fabric`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-002-smu_axi_atomic_operation_test]]
===== 2 `smu_axi_atomic_operation_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_axi_atomic_operation_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`

*Objective*: Prove a non-ATOP SMN read completes with RID==ARID; ATOP is not drivable here.

*Test Coverage*:

* smu_axi_atomic_operation_test - non-ATOP SMN read path on the wrapper.
* `tb/tb_wrapper_top.sv` ties `smu_axi_in_req.aw.atop` to `'0`, the flat `s_axi` port list carries no ATOP pin, and the package has no ATOP driver, so no ATOP transaction reaches the DUT.
* This test makes no claim about ATOP rejection; a green run here is not ATOP non-support credit.
* What it proves: with the SMC aperture and an INBOUND0 window programmed over JTAG, a plain 32-bit read of `VERSION_LO` on the SMN path is answered OKAY with the register's reset value, and the RID sampled off the live R channel mirrors the ARID issued.
* DECERR / err_slv poison deny semantics stay OUT for this leaf: no ATOP can be driven to provoke them.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Run the test body `cocotb_wrapper/tests/smu_axi_atomic_operation_test.py` over the wrapper's product interfaces; it drives the TAP and pins directly and has no separate sequence file.
. Compare every access against its stated expectation in the scoreboard.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-AXI-NON-ATOP` |Evidence token `AXI_NON_ATOP_OK`: non-ATOP SMN path completes
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `fabric`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-008-smu_axi_id_width_conversion_test]]
===== 8 `smu_axi_id_width_conversion_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_axi_id_width_conversion_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip` master on the flat `ext_in_*` pins; `ocah_jtag_vip` to open the INBOUND0 window; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: An 8-bit-ID `ext_in` read reaches an SMC register through the SEP=1 crossbar and returns OKAY with RID == ARID and the RDL reset value.

*Test Coverage*:

* The wrapper elaborates SEP=1, so `ext_in` traffic is routed by `smu_axi_xbar` to the SMC inbound subordinate (`hw/sys/smu/doc/index.adoc`, "AXI Crossbar", row `ext_in` -> `smc_in`). The `ext_in_*` pins carry 8-bit transaction IDs (`tb/tb_wrapper_top.sv`) and the SMC system input carries 6-bit ones (`doc/integrator/src/smu.adoc`, "AXI Interface Configuration"), so a RID that returns on `ext_in_rid` equal to the issued ARID has crossed the fabric's ID handling in both directions.
* Three probes on one `CHIP_CONFIG` register page, each under a distinct seed-drawn ARID, must complete OKAY with RID == ARID, and the `VERSION_LO` probe must return its RDL reset value. The error slave that answers an unprogrammed SYS_IN also echoes the ARID, so OKAY together with the reset value is what distinguishes the register route from that slave.
* The SEP=0 direct ID converter (`gen_no_sep`) is not elaborated on this bench and is not claimed here; no target of this package covers it, and the names that prove it (`smu_axi_external_port_connectivity_test`, `smu_axi_xbar_structure_test`) are `not-runnable` in `hw/sys/smu/doc/dv/SMU_DEFERRED_DISPOSITION.adoc`.
* Deny-path (DECERR / err_slv poison) is out of scope here: claiming DECERR without the allow control in the same run would violate NEGATIVE-NEEDS-POSITIVE-CONTROL.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Route the SMC local aperture and open an INBOUND0 window over the probed page through JTAG2AXI, checking each write's status.
. Issue one 32-bit read per probe address from `ext_in` under a distinct ARID; require RRESP == OKAY and the sampled RID == ARID for each.
. Read `VERSION_LO` again and require its RDL reset value.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-AXI-ID-WIDTH` |Evidence token `AXI_ID_WIDTH_OK`: every `ext_in` probe completes OKAY with RID == ARID through the SEP=1 crossbar, and `VERSION_LO` returns its RDL reset value
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `fabric`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-012-smu_otp_vs_fabric_map_race_test]]
===== 12 `smu_otp_vs_fabric_map_race_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_otp_vs_fabric_map_race_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`

*Objective*: OTP then fabric JTAG2AXI writes to the same MAP SPARE[0] word back to back: the later writer wins on both read paths and in the shadow register, with no torn value. The `_race_` in the test name is historical and overstates this; the concurrency case it suggests is carried as a waived row in `hw/sys/smu/doc/dv/SMU_DEFERRED_DISPOSITION.adoc`, with the reason no stimulus on this bench can produce it.

*Test Coverage*:

* Both writers are JTAG2AXI bridges behind the one primary TAP, so their DR shifts are serialised and the OTP write has completed before the fabric write can be issued; the sequence records the OTP status at its first capture after the kick and requires it complete before issuing the fabric write. No overlap between the two writes is claimed.
* The fabric write to MAP SPARE[0] must then be the value read back over the OTP bridge, over the fabric bridge, and in the shadow register (last writer wins), followed by an alternating OTP/fabric ping-pong with the same last-writer rule.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Drive `smu_otp_vs_fabric_map_race_test_seq` over the wrapper's product interfaces.
. Compare every access against its stated expectation in the scoreboard.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-OTPFAB-GATE-OPEN` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-OTPFAB-B2B-ISSUED` |OTP write completed, then the fabric write completed
|`CHK-OTPFAB-B2B-COHERENT` |OTP-bridge and fabric-bridge readbacks both equal the later (fabric) pattern
|`CHK-OTPFAB-B2B-SHADOW` |shadow-map word equals that readback
|`CHK-OTPFAB-PINGPONG` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-RACE-OTP-FAB` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|assertion |otp_vs_fabric race incomplete s1=<value> s2=<value> s3=<value>
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `dtp_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-016-smu_xtrig_ctm_remap_test]]

*Objective*: CTM external remap via product pins only.

*Test Coverage*:

* xtrig_ctm_dst_req[7:0] -> dtp_xtrig_ctm_dst_req[9:2]; SMC[1:0] stay 0
* xtrig_ctm_dst_ack[7] follows dst_req[7] within a settle window and holds: lane 7 is the one lane the TB configures for req/ack handshaking (`XTRIG_INT_CT_MODE = 8'h80`). The integrator guide states that a mode-0 (pulse-synchronised) lane's ack is unused and pins no level for it, so lanes 0-6 are recorded in the log, not compared
* xtrig_ctm_src_ack[7:0] -> dtp_xtrig_ctm_src_ack[9:2]; ack[1:0] hardwired 0
* TB xtrig_ctm_src_req stays idle (0) while only ack is driven (no DTP peer)

*Test Procedure*:

. Run the test body `cocotb_wrapper/tests/smu_xtrig_ctm_remap_test.py` over the wrapper's product interfaces; it drives the TAP and pins directly and has no separate sequence file.
. Compare every access against its stated expectation in the scoreboard.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-XT-CTM-REMAP` |Evidence token `XT_CTM_REMAP`: dst_req[7:0] lands on the DTP lanes [9:2] with the SMC lanes idle, src_ack[7:0] lands on [9:2] with [1:0] hardwired 0, and ack[7] follows req[7] on the handshake lane within the settle window; the pulse-synchronised lanes' ack levels are observed, not compared
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `dtp_under_smu`, `hosted`.

[[sec-017-smu_axi_in_burst_outstanding_test]]
===== 17 `smu_axi_in_burst_outstanding_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_axi_in_burst_outstanding_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip` master on the flat `ext_in_*` pins; `ocah_jtag_vip` to open the INBOUND0 window; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: The inbound SMN port carries several transfers in flight and refuses the burst types the register targets do not support.

*Test Coverage*:

* With the SMC local aperture routed and an INBOUND0 window opened over JTAG2AXI, sixty-four reads of `VERSION_LO` under distinct ARIDs are launched while RREADY is held low. The AR handshakes the port accepts before its first R handshake are counted on the `ext_in_*` pins, and at least two must be accepted: a port that serialises accepts one and fails here. Every read must then return the VERSION_LO RDL reset value with OKAY, so a dropped transfer fails on its bounded wait.
* Sixty-four writes under one AWID are launched against `SCRATCH_COLD` while BREADY is held low; the AW handshakes accepted before the first B handshake are counted the same way and at least two are required. The last value written is what a later read returns, which is the ordering AXI requires of same-ID transfers.
* No specification document states the inbound port's outstanding depth (`doc/integrator/src/smu.adoc` "SMU Default Parameters" sizes no such queue), so the floor of two is a DV-owned rule and the accepted count the port reaches is logged with each depth check rather than compared against a stated depth.
* A four-beat 32-bit INCR burst read from VERSION_LO, covering the four CHIP_CONFIG registers, and one written to the first four SCRATCH_COLD words are OKAY, and a WRAP burst read and a FIXED burst write of the same address, length and size are refused with SLVERR, so the refusal follows the burst type and not the length.
* Spec gap: no SMC chapter states how the register path answers a non-incrementing or a multi-beat INCR burst (`hw/sys/smc/doc/fabric.adoc` names only the decode error of a deny entry); the SLVERR and OKAY expectations are the documentation item.
* S3 takes the same window into the SMC SPM (`SMC_TOP_SPM_MEMORY_BASE_ADDR`/`_SIZE` in `smc_addr.h`): every AxSIZE with each of address bits [2:0] set and cleared between transfers, and a 32-bit write and read at every address bit the SPM leaves free, read back what was written; an eight-beat INCR burst reads back its beats, and a WRAP write followed by an INCR write complete, the INCR one OKAY.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Route the SMC local aperture and open an INBOUND0 window over JTAG2AXI, checking each write's status.
. Arm an AR/R accept counter on the `ext_in_*` pins, hold RREADY low, issue sixty-four reads of `VERSION_LO` with distinct ARIDs; require at least two AR handshakes before the first R handshake, then compare each data word and response.
. Arm an AW/B accept counter, hold BREADY low, issue sixty-four writes to `SCRATCH_COLD` under one AWID; require at least two AW handshakes before the first B handshake, then read the register back and compare against the last value written.
. Issue an INCR burst read and write at the register targets and require OKAY, then a WRAP burst read and a FIXED multi-beat burst write of the same shape and require SLVERR on each.
. Write and read the SPM at every size cell and address-walk cell, write and read an INCR burst, then issue a WRAP write and an INCR write.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-AXIIN-DEPTH` |the inbound port accepts at least two address handshakes before it returns the first response, on the read train and on the write train, and every read returns the VERSION_LO RDL reset value with OKAY
|`CHK-AXIIN-DEPTH-ORDER` |sixty-four inbound writes in flight under one AWID leave the last value written in SCRATCH_COLD
|`CHK-AXIIN-BURST` |an inbound INCR burst read and write at the register targets are OKAY, and a WRAP burst read of the same shape is refused with SLVERR
|`CHK-AXIIN-BURST-FIXED` |an inbound multi-beat FIXED burst write at a register target is refused with SLVERR
|`CHK-AXIIN-SPM-SWEEP` |every SPM size and address cell reads back OKAY with the bytes written, the INCR burst reads back its beats, and the INCR write after the WRAP write is OKAY
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `fabric`, `hosted`.

'''''
[[sec-018-smu_xbar_connectivity_matrix_test]]
===== 18 `smu_xbar_connectivity_matrix_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_xbar_connectivity_matrix_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip` master on the flat `ext_in_*` pins; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: An `ext_in` access that matches neither programmed aperture is answered DECERR by the crossbar and never reaches `ext_out`.

*Test Coverage*:

* The rule is the integrator guide's (`doc/integrator/src/smu.adoc`, "SMU AXI Crossbar Address Map": unmatched inbound requests return a decode error and do not reach `ext_out`); the SEP and SMC apertures are read live from the DUT to pick an address outside both.
* A read and two writes are issued, the second write with every AWID bit set; the crossbar itself must answer DECERR with the issued IDs, and the SMN egress boundary counters must not move.
* SMU-XBAR-CONN.S5 and S6 (the SEP and SMC self-routes) need SEP or SMC firmware that targets its own aperture and are not claimed here.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read the live SEP and SMC aperture registers and choose a candidate address outside both.
. Sample the `ext_out` egress boundary counters, issue a read and two writes at that address, the second with the all-ones AWID, and require DECERR with the issued IDs.
. Re-sample the egress counters after a settle and require them unchanged.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-XBAR-CONN-S7` |unmatched ext_in read and writes, including the all-ones AWID, DECERR with the issued IDs and nothing reaches ext_out
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''
[[sec-019-smu_periph_ext_window_test]]
===== 19 `smu_periph_ext_window_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_periph_ext_window_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (SMC fabric JTAG2AXI); `ocah_axi_vip` for the out-of-chiplet leg; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: The SMC adopter external window is reachable over JTAG2AXI at the addresses the SMC memory map and the register map generated from `smc.rdl` give, answers at the two blocks those sources place, returns DECERR on the first page above everything they allocate, and shrinks with the SMC aperture.

*Test Coverage*:

* EFUSE_SHIM_CTRL.EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME, at the EFUSE_SHIM_CTRL block base the generated SMC map gives, is read at its RDL reset value over the eFuse bank-control AXI-Lite port, written, read back and restored, so the shim window is proven both ways.
* The straps pair in the supplementary region answers, and the first 4 KiB page above every allocation the sources record, still inside the window, returns DECERR.
* REGION_SIZE shrunk so the local aperture ends exactly at the window sends the same shim read out of the chiplet instead, observed on the outbound boundary rather than inferred from the response alone. REGION_SIZE zero is not driven: `fabric.adoc` forbids firmware from programming it, and the crossbar's address decoder assumes every rule spans at least one byte, so a zero-size aperture is outside the design's contract rather than a state to prove.
* Golden sources: the window base and size from `hw/sys/smc/regs/gen/c/smc_addr.h` (`SMC_TOP_SMC_EXTERNAL_*`) and the mandatory / supplementary split from `hw/sys/smc/doc/memmap.adoc` ("AXI-Lite External Window"); the EFUSE_SHIM_CTRL block base, both region bases and the allocated extent from the register map generated from `smc.rdl` for the boot ROM (`hw/sys/smc/bootrom/prod/registers/smc_top_regs.h`); the EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME offset and reset from the PeakRDL headers of `hw/ip/efuse/dv/models/regs/efuse_shim_ctrl.rdl`; the straps addresses transcribed from `hw/sys/smc/bootrom/prod/doc/hardware-initialization.adoc` ("Straps and eFuses"), with `straps.rdl` fixing STRAPS_HI at STRAPS_LO + 4; the REGION_SIZE and LOCAL_BASE resets from `smc_base_config.h`. The sequence refuses to run if these sources disagree about the window.
* Specification gap: `hw/sys/smc/doc/memmap.adoc` leaves every placement inside the window to the adopter and `hw/ip/efuse/doc/memmap.adoc` says the SHIM sits at no fixed address, so the shim base rests on the generated boot-ROM map (which describes a vendor shim layout; only the block base is taken from it, and the register inside follows `efuse_shim_ctrl.rdl`) and the straps address on the boot-ROM documentation. Neither is an SMC memory-map statement. The map passes the unallocated remainder through to the adopter external port without saying what answers it; `hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc` ties an unused port to DECERR, and the DV rule is that an address no source allocates must not be answered by a live register. `CHK-PERIPH-EXT-UNMAPPED` is graded against that rule, not against a decode boundary a document states.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Over JTAG2AXI read, write, read back and restore EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME at the EFUSE_SHIM_CTRL base the generated SMC map gives.
. Read STRAPS_LO and STRAPS_HI at the boot-ROM documented address, then the first page above every recorded allocation, and compare the responses.
. Shrink REGION_SIZE so LOCAL_BASE + size ends at the window and repeat the shim read; require the transaction on the outbound boundary.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-PERIPH-EXT-SHIM` |EFUSE_SHIM_CTRL.EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME, at the block base the generated SMC map gives, reads its RDL reset value over the eFuse bank-control AXI-Lite port, takes a written value and is restored
|`CHK-PERIPH-EXT-STRAPS` |STRAPS_LO and STRAPS_HI at the boot-ROM documented supplementary-region address both answer SUCCESS
|`CHK-PERIPH-EXT-UNMAPPED` |the first page above every allocation the sources record, still inside the window, DECERRs (DV rule; see the specification gap above)
|`CHK-PERIPH-EXT-APERTURE` |REGION_SIZE shrunk so the local aperture ends at the window sends the same shim read out of the chiplet instead
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `smc_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''
==== RESET

[[sec-020-smu_boot_stall_jtag_cold_reset_matrix_test]]
===== 20 `smu_boot_stall_jtag_cold_reset_matrix_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_boot_stall_jtag_cold_reset_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, `ocah_jtag_vip`

*Objective*: Boot-stall cold-reset sticky + TRST clear matrix.

*Test Coverage*:

* Stall asserted across cold reset (TRST held high) stays sticky; fuse gated
* TRST pulse (reset_tap) clears DEBUG_CONTROL / boot-stall exports
* fuse_reset releases after TRST clear
* Sticky: re-assert stall without primary reset does not re-gate fuse_reset

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Run without `+skip_fuse_sense` (the `testlists/all.toml` entry carries none): the SMC eFuse
  controller senses the `+smc_efuse_hex` image through the wrapper's eFuse model, and
  `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` is that controller's own sense-complete release. The run log
  carries `Not skipping fuse sense` and no shadow-preload load.
. Run the test body `cocotb_wrapper/tests/smu_boot_stall_jtag_cold_reset_matrix_test.py` over the wrapper's product interfaces; it drives the TAP and pins directly and has no separate sequence file.
. Watch `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` 0 -> 1 after every primary reset (bounded at 5000 `clk_smu`; a
  flag already high, or an expiry, fails) before any `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` compare. At
  bring-up the release must then follow within 256 `clk_smu`. With the stall held across the cold
  reset the gate must read `0` on every one of the 64 `clk_smu` after that rise, so the `0` is the
  gate and not a sense still in progress. After the stall clears, with the sense already done, the
  release must arrive within 256 `clk_smu` (the `prim_sync3` + sticky flop + 16-stage pipe path,
  not the sense latency) and stay released for 64 `clk_smu`.
. Compare every access against its stated expectation in the scoreboard.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

The scenario has two implementations that share this claim: the cocotb test body
`smu_boot_stall_jtag_cold_reset_matrix_test.py` and the SV-UVM
`smu_boot_stall_jtag_cold_reset_matrix_test_seq.svh` (`--framework uvm`). The SV-UVM
shape runs the same eight legs per pass on the real sense (the testlist entry
carries no `+skip_fuse_sense` in either framework), including the GPIO-pad legs
(pad alone, both sources, override low over the pad), with the sense-done and
fuse-reset observables read through `smu_tb_if` and every bounded wait recorded.
Independently of the sequence, `smu_debug_control_tdr_ref_model` predicts the
capture of every DEBUG_CONTROL scan from the register shadow and the TAP resets it
observes, and `smu_boot_gate_ref_model` requires the boot gate open and the sense
complete at every fuse-reset release `smu_reset_pin_monitor` observes.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-STALL-STICKY` |Evidence token `STALL_COLD_STICKY`: stall survives cold; the sensed `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` stays 0 for 64 `clk_smu` with `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` high
|`CHK-STALL-TRST` |Evidence token `STALL_TRST_CLEAR`: TRST clears stall; with the sense already done, the release follows the clear within the gate path and stays released
|`CHK-STALL-REASSERT` |Evidence token `STALL_REASSERT_STICKY`: re-assert sticky
|`CHK-STALL-BASELINE` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): after the TAP reset the fuse reset is released, the sense is done and the stall override idle
|`CHK-DEBUG-CONTROL-READBACK` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the written DEBUG_CONTROL image reads back on its four software-owned bits and on the SMC exports
|`CHK-STALL-STICKY` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): `STALL_COLD_STICKY` in the SV-UVM shape: the stall exports survive the cold reset and the fuse reset reads 0 on every hold-window clock after the sense completes
|`CHK-STALL-TRST` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): `STALL_TRST_CLEAR` in the SV-UVM shape: with the sense done and the gate shut, the TRST pulse clears both exports, the release follows within the gate-path bound and holds, and the release latency counted from the instant the clear stimulus began fits inside the hold window the sticky claim rests on
|`CHK-STALL-REASSERT` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): `STALL_REASSERT_STICKY` in the SV-UVM shape: the re-asserted stall does not re-gate the released fuse reset
|`CHK-STALL-PAD-ONLY` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the GPIO pad alone gates the fuse reset across a cold reset while the JTAG override is idle
|`CHK-STALL-BOTH-SOURCES` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): pad and JTAG stall asserted together keep the gate shut
|`CHK-STALL-OVRD-MASKS-PAD` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the override forced low over the driving pad opens the gate within the gate-path bound and holds, and the release latency counted from the end of the clearing DR scan fits inside the hold window
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): eight bounded waits (the TAP-state confirmation, the baseline release, two sense completions, two primary releases, two gate releases) each name a finite bound and their last state; none expired
|`CHK-SB-MIN-ACTIVITY` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the `debug_control_tdr` feature compares at least the seven scans the pass issues and `boot_gate` at least the two releases it causes, both counts from the always-on scoreboard
|`CHK-SB-DEBUG-CONTROL-TDR` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): always-on scoreboard, feature `debug_control_tdr`: `smu_debug_control_tdr_ref_model` predicts every DEBUG_CONTROL capture from the previous update and zero after any TAP reset (TRST, Test-Logic-Reset, power-on reset), the TCK-domain register untouched by the cold reset
|`CHK-SB-BOOT-GATE` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): always-on scoreboard, feature `boot_gate`: `smu_boot_gate_ref_model` requires `ext_boot_seq_done_i` = 1 and `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` = 1 in the SMU clock of every `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` 0 -> 1 the pin monitor observes
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `dtp_under_smu`, `hosted`. Under `--framework uvm` the SV-UVM rows above govern instead, and `+SMU_DEBUG_CONTROL_SCOREBOARD_NEGATIVE` and `+SMU_BOOT_GATE_SCOREBOARD_NEGATIVE` must each fail the run.

'''''

[[sec-021-smu_boot_stall_vs_ic_reset_priority_test]]
===== 21 `smu_boot_stall_vs_ic_reset_priority_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_boot_stall_vs_ic_reset_priority_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip`, `ocah_jtag_vip`

*Objective*: Stall sticky isolation vs IC_RESET; TRST clears both.

*Test Coverage*:

* IC_RESET warm then cold while stall sticky: ovrd asserts; stall survives; the cold override asserts the SMC primary reset
* IC_RESET DEFAULT: domain ovrd clears; the primary reset releases again and the still-sticky stall gates fuse_reset across that reset too
* TRST clears stall AND IC_RESET; fuse_reset releases

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Run without `+skip_fuse_sense` (the `testlists/all.toml` entry carries none): the SMC eFuse
  controller senses the `+smc_efuse_hex` image through the wrapper's eFuse model, and
  `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` is that controller's own sense-complete release. The run log
  carries `Not skipping fuse sense` and no shadow-preload load.
. Run the test body `cocotb_wrapper/tests/smu_boot_stall_vs_ic_reset_priority_test.py` over the wrapper's product interfaces; it drives the TAP and pins directly and has no separate sequence file.
. Watch `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` 0 -> 1 after every primary reset (bounded at 5000 `clk_smu`; a
  flag already high, or an expiry, fails) before any `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` compare. At
  bring-up the release must then follow within 256 `clk_smu`. With the stall held across the cold
  reset the gate must read `0` on every one of the 64 `clk_smu` after that rise, so the `0` is the
  gate and not a sense still in progress. After the stall clears, with the sense already done, the
  release must arrive within 256 `clk_smu` (the `prim_sync3` + sticky flop + 16-stage pipe path,
  not the sense latency) and stay released for 64 `clk_smu`.
. Compare every access against its stated expectation in the scoreboard.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-STALL-VS-IC` |Evidence token `STALL_VS_IC_RESET`: IC_RESET does not clear stall; the sensed `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` stays gated through the IC_RESET legs (sampled after `smc_fuse_sense_done_o`) and releases within the gate path after TRST
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `dtp_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-024-smu_ext_boot_seq_gate_test]]
===== 24 `smu_ext_boot_seq_gate_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_ext_boot_seq_gate_test`

*BFM / Model*: TB pin drive only (no external VIP); `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: SMU_006: ext_boot_seq_done_i gates smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o release.

*Test Coverage*:

* Sequence for smu_ext_boot_seq_gate_test (SMU_006).
* Observes smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o — the RTL consumer gated by ext_boot_seq_done_i (port_table: gates reset release).
* rst_primary_smc_clk_no is NOT gated by this pin (negative control CHK-PRIMARY-NOT-GATED).
* With the pin lowered again across a cold reset, the gate holds the fuse reset asserted after the new release, and raising the pin releases it.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Drive `smu_ext_boot_seq_gate_test_seq` over the wrapper's product interfaces.
. Compare every access against its stated expectation in the scoreboard.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

The scenario has two implementations that share this claim: the cocotb
`smu_ext_boot_seq_gate_test_seq.py` and the SV-UVM `smu_ext_boot_seq_gate_test_seq.svh`
(`--framework uvm`). The SV-UVM shape re-runs the cold reset with the gate shut at
the start of every pass (the base test's bring-up releases with the gate open), so
the gated window, the negative control and the ungate are proved once per pass, and
stamps the first primary and fuse rises, and the gate level in the clock of the primary
rise, with a tracker armed while the cold reset holds both low, so the negative control
and the fence are taken from DUT edges rather than from levels found after the settle.
Independently of the sequence,
`smu_boot_gate_ref_model` requires the boot gate open and the sense complete at
every fuse-reset release `smu_reset_pin_monitor` observes, so a release inside the
gated window fails the scoreboard as well as the sequence.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-PRIMARY-NOT-GATED` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-BOOT-SEQ-GATE` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |Both bounded waits (primary release while gated; fuse release after ungate) completed within RELEASE_BOUND clk_smu_i cycles and none expired: the compare is the list of (label, expired, bound) records against the expected list, so a missing, expired or differently bounded wait fails
|`CHK-NONVAC` |Ordered fence from DUT edges, simulation-time stamped: the first rise of `rst_primary_smc_clk_no` precedes the `ext_boot_seq_done_i` drive, and the first rise of `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` follows both the end of the gated sample window and the ungate drive; a fuse release inside the gated or pre-ungate window fails
|`CHK-BOOT-SEQ-REGATE` |with `ext_boot_seq_done_i` low across a second cold reset, `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` stays low for the whole gated sample window after the release and rises within RELEASE_BOUND cycles of raising the pin
|`CHK-BOOT-SEQ-GATE` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the sense is done before the gated window, the gate reads 0 and the fuse reset reads 0 on every one of the 64 gated samples, and the fuse reset releases within the gate-path bound after the ungate
|`CHK-PRIMARY-NOT-GATED` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the primary reset is low under the cold reset, its 0 -> 1 is observed by the tracker with the gate reading 0 in that clock, and it reads 1 within the bound while the gate still reads 0
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the three bounded waits (the sense done before the gated window, the primary release while gated, the fuse release after the ungate within the gate-path bound) name a finite bound and their last level; none expired
|`CHK-NONVAC` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the DUT-edge fence, simulation-time stamped: first primary rise before the ungate, first fuse rise after the gated window's end and after the ungate
|`CHK-SB-MIN-ACTIVITY` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the `boot_gate` feature compares at least the one release the pass causes, from the always-on scoreboard
|`CHK-SB-BOOT-GATE` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): always-on scoreboard, feature `boot_gate`: `smu_boot_gate_ref_model` requires `ext_boot_seq_done_i` = 1 and `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` = 1 in the SMU clock of every `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` 0 -> 1 the pin monitor observes
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `smc_under_smu`, `smoke`, `hosted`. Under `--framework uvm` the SV-UVM rows above govern instead, and `+SMU_BOOT_GATE_SCOREBOARD_NEGATIVE` must fail the run.

'''''

[[sec-028-smu_jtag_reset_override_test]]
===== 28 `smu_jtag_reset_override_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_jtag_reset_override_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`

*Objective*: IC_RESET override/release on EXT and SMC cold-reset slices.

*Test Coverage*:

* The TDR is 155 bits on this wrapper: 68 SMC + 8 SEP + 1 EXT ports, two bits each, plus `reset_hold` (`doc/integrator/src/smu.adoc` "IC_RESET TDR Structure"). The EXT port sits nearest TDO, so a helper geometry short of the TDR lands the EXT fields in the SEP slice and the EXT leg below fails; this is the leaf that catches a wrong slice width.
* Default IC_RESET readback is all-ones over the whole DR, with TDO required resolvable on every readback: the JTAG VIP reads an X/Z TDO bit as 0, so during each IC_RESET readback the leaf samples the bench's `jtag_tdo` on every TCK rising edge while `jtag_tdo_oen` (high in Shift-IR and Shift-DR) is high, and an X/Z bit, an X/Z enable, or fewer driven bits than the 155-bit DR fails the run. Verilator is two-state, so there the guard cannot fire; it bites on a four-state simulator.
* The EXT override is applied and released in two Update-DRs each, `reset_control` first and `reset_enable` second, the order the DTP JTAG chapter (`hw/sys/dtp/doc/jtag.adoc`) gives so a hazard-free consumer multiplexer never sees its select and data move together. `.ovrd` is active high and `.val` carries `reset_control`, so each staging update is visible on the EXT slice alone.
* EXT control=0 staged under enable=1 drives ctrl_n=0 with ovrd still 0; enable=0 then asserts ovrd=1 with ctrl_n=0 while the whole override half of both the SMC and the SEP slice reads 0; control=1 staged under enable=0 drives ctrl_n=1 with ovrd still 1.
* SMC cold_reset port override updates the hierarchical SMC slice while the EXT override is released: the SMC slice's override half reads exactly the cold-reset bit, the SEP slice's override half reads 0 and the EXT override reads 0.
* Clearing the TDR restores both overrides to 0.
* Every managed-subsystem cold and warm port and every SEP slice port but the Key Manager's is then walked in the same two-update order: control staged under enable=1, enable cleared to apply the override, control released under the override, then the TDR cleared. After each update, the clearing one included, the TDR reads back as written, `ic_reset_smc_ovrd_o` carries exactly the ovrd bits of the SS ports, and the SMC and SEP slices are read whole: each is a packed struct whose `.ovrd` half sits above its `.val` half, the first field of each half nearest TDI, carrying the inverse of reset_enable and reset_control (`doc/integrator/src/smu.adoc`, "IC_RESET TDR Structure"; fuse is the SMC port nearest TDO, the Key Manager the SEP port nearest TDO). Every port's override and value are compared: override on the applied ports only, value equal to the control written, and every other port at its default.
* The Key Manager port (`km_jtag_rst_n`, the SEP port nearest the external slice) has its control staged and released without the override being applied. With the override applied and lifted, the Key Manager's ROM request (`hw/top/sep_ip_integration.sv` `u_km_rom.req_i`) reads X on a four-state simulator and `prim_rom`'s `noXOnCsI` assertion fires; that is reported as an open design question rather than walked here.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Run the test body `cocotb_wrapper/tests/smu_jtag_reset_override_test.py` over the wrapper's product interfaces; it drives the TAP and pins directly and has no separate sequence file.
. Compare every access against its stated expectation in the scoreboard.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

The scenario has two implementations that share this claim: the cocotb test body
`smu_jtag_reset_override_test.py` and the SV-UVM `smu_jtag_reset_override_test_seq.svh`
(`--framework uvm`). The SV-UVM shape holds the TDR geometry as a DV-owned table
transcribed from the integrator guide (68 SMC ports, 8 SEP ports, one external port,
two bits each plus `reset_hold`), scans the 155-bit register through the wide DR path,
and reads the override exports and the whole SMC slice (`jtag_smc_reset_ctrl.ovrd`
/ `.val`) through `smu_tb_if`; it also confirms the SMC primary reset releases again
once the cold override is cleared, so the next pass starts from the same baseline.
Independently of the sequence, `smu_ic_reset_tdr_ref_model` predicts the capture of
every IC_RESET scan from the register shadow it rebuilds from the updates and the
TAP resets it observes, and the always-on scoreboard pairs the two.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-IC-DEFAULT` |Evidence token `IC_RESET_DEFAULT`: IC_RESET default all-ones, every driven TDO bit of the scan resolvable
|`CHK-IC-EXT-STAGED` |Evidence token `IC_RESET_EXT_STAGED`: EXT control staged low reaches `jtag_ic_reset_ext_o.val` while `.ovrd` stays 0
|`CHK-IC-EXT-RELEASE-STAGED` |Evidence token `IC_RESET_EXT_RELEASE_STAGED`: EXT control staged high reaches `.val` while `.ovrd` stays 1
|`CHK-IC-DOMAIN` |Evidence token `IC_RESET_DOMAIN_EXCL`: with the EXT override applied, the EXT override is 1 and the SMC and SEP slices' whole override halves are 0; with the SMC cold override applied, the SMC override half is exactly the cold-reset bit and the EXT override and the SEP override half are 0
|`CHK-IC-SS-SEP-WALK` |Evidence token `IC_RESET_SS_SEP_WALK`: the SS and SEP ports staged, applied, released and cleared, with every SMC and SEP slice port's override and value following each update
|`CHK-IC-DEFAULT` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the reset image (all ones over the whole 155-bit register) reads back and both override exports are idle
|`CHK-IC-EXT-OVERRIDE` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the external port overridden with reset_control 0 asserts `jtag_ic_reset_ext_ovrd` and drives `jtag_ic_reset_ext_ctrl_n` low, within a bounded wait
|`CHK-IC-READBACK` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the written EXT image reads back whole
|`CHK-IC-SMC-COLD-OVERRIDE` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the SMC cold port overridden likewise asserts the SMC cold override, drives it low, and the SMC slice the reset controller receives (`ovrd` and `val` vectors) equals the slice the image encodes; the SMC primary reset is held low while the override stands
|`CHK-IC-DOMAIN` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): while one slice is overridden the other's export stays idle
|`CHK-IC-CLEAR` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the reset image restores both exports and the SMC primary reset releases again
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): seven bounded waits (the TAP-state confirmation, the four export transitions, the primary reset held by the cold override, the primary release) each name a finite bound and their last state; none expired
|`CHK-SB-MIN-ACTIVITY` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the `ic_reset_tdr` feature's comparison count, from the always-on scoreboard, is at or above the five scans the pass issues
|`CHK-SB-IC-RESET-TDR` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): always-on scoreboard, feature `ic_reset_tdr`: `smu_ic_reset_tdr_ref_model` predicts every IC_RESET capture from the previous update, the reset image after TRST or power-on reset, and after Test-Logic-Reset the reset image only while `reset_hold` is 1 (PTAP document, IC_RESET fields). A required feature with zero comparisons fails the run
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `dtp_under_smu`, `hosted`. Under `--framework uvm` the SV-UVM rows above govern instead, and `+SMU_IC_RESET_SCOREBOARD_NEGATIVE` must fail the run.

'''''
