[[sec-059-smu_jtag_chain_enhanced_test]]
===== 59 `smu_jtag_chain_enhanced_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_jtag_chain_enhanced_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`

*Objective*: PTAP IDCODE + BYPASS after VIP TCK period reprogram; no Force.

*Test Coverage*:

* PTAP IDCODE + BYPASS at 1/5/10/20 MHz TCK (SEP=1, no Force).
* S1: TAP ready at the default period (IDCODE).
* S2: For each programmed VIP TCK period, IDCODE matches the IEEE packing.
* S3: At each period, BYPASS is a one-bit register (8-bit TDI, TDO is capture-0 then TDI delayed one bit).
* S4: Completeness vs the independent required set (labels + programmed VIP `tck_period_ns`).
* A missing `FREQ_NS` leg or a plusarg that pins every TAP to one period fails here.
* Does not claim analog setup/hold vs silicon; FAIL-ON is wrong IDCODE, BYPASS delay, missing required frequency, or VIP period not matching the required nanosecond set.
* Not claimed: Force feat_ctrl; JTAG2AXI.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Drive `smu_jtag_chain_enhanced_test_seq` over the wrapper's product interfaces.
. Compare every access against its stated expectation in the scoreboard.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-JTAG-CHAIN-READY` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-JTAG-CHAIN-FREQ` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-JTAG-IDCODE` |Evidence token `JTAG_IDCODE_OK`: IDCODE reads the default DTP IDCODE at every swept TCK period
|`CHK-JTAG-BYPASS` |Evidence token `JTAG_BYPASS_OK`: BYPASS returns the expected shifted pattern at every swept TCK period
|`CHK-JTAG-CHAIN-ENHANCED` |Evidence token `JTAG_CHAIN_ENHANCED_OK`: all required TCK labels ran at their programmed periods
|assertion |jtag_chain_enhanced incomplete s1=<value> s2=<value>
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `dtp_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-060-smu_smc_dtp_jtag2axi_smoke_test]]
===== 60 `smu_smc_dtp_jtag2axi_smoke_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_dtp_jtag2axi_smoke_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`

*Objective*: SMC fabric J2A SCRATCH_15 + SPM + series INCR; gate open on the SEP=1 wrapper.

*Test Coverage*:

* SMC fabric JTAG2AXI smoke: gate open, CAPS, SCRATCH_15, SPM, series INCR.
* S1: After TCK sync, `tb_smc_jtag2axi_security_disable` is 0 and `SMC_JTAG2AXI_CAPS` matches the RTL 14-bit packing.
* S2: SINGLE_OP 32b write/readback of CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_15 (not SCRATCH_0 — that mailbox is the live boot ROM PASS magic).
* S3: SINGLE_OP 64b write/readback of SPM base.
* S4: Series DATA_INCR write then readback at SPM+0x40 — data match.
* Addresses from `smc_addr.h`.
* Not claimed: Force-closed gate; OTP J2A; unmapped DECERR; NO_INCR / WITH_ERROR_STATUS series modes.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Drive `smu_smc_dtp_jtag2axi_smoke_test_seq` over the wrapper's product interfaces.
. Compare every access against its stated expectation in the scoreboard.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

The scenario has two implementations that share this claim: the cocotb
`smu_smc_dtp_jtag2axi_smoke_test_seq.py` and the SV-UVM
`smu_smc_dtp_jtag2axi_smoke_test_seq.svh` (`--framework uvm`). The SV-UVM shape packs
and decodes every bridge register through the `dtp_env_pkg` codec the DTP bench
uses for this bridge, takes the expected CAPS word from the integrator guide's
default parameters and the addresses from the generated `smc_top_addrmap_pkg`, runs
the directed patterns
on pass 0 and seeded patterns on the later passes, and raises the TCK period to at
least four SMU clocks (the cocotb `min_jtag_smu_ratio` clamp). Independently of the
sequence, the embedded DTP's `dtp_jtag2axi_req_ref_model` pairs every transaction a
passive monitor sees on the DTP's SMC debug port with the request the scan encoded,
and `dtp_jtag2axi_status_ref_model` pairs every SINGLE_OP and SERIES_CTRL capture
with the completion the bus returned.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-JTAG2AXI-SMOKE-JTAG-READY` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-JTAG2AXI-SMOKE-GATE-OPEN` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-JTAG2AXI-SMOKE-SCRATCH` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-JTAG2AXI-SMOKE-SPM` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|`CHK-JTAG2AXI-SMOKE-SERIES-INCR` |Evidence token, emitted only after the compares it names have held
|assertion |jtag2axi_smoke incomplete s1=<value> s2=<value> s3=<value> s4=<value>
|`CHK-JTAG2AXI-SMOKE-JTAG-READY` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): IDCODE equals the SMU-configured value after the TAP reset
|`CHK-JTAG2AXI-SMOKE-GATE-OPEN` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the bridge's lifecycle gate reads 0 and SMC_JTAG2AXI_CAPS equals the geometry the integrator guide's default parameters state (rd/wr pipeline depth 3, 8-byte data, 56-bit address, AXI4)
|`CHK-JTAG2AXI-SMOKE-SCRATCH` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): SINGLE_OP 32-bit write and readback of CPU_CTRL SCRATCH_15, both SUCCESS, data equal
|`CHK-JTAG2AXI-SMOKE-SPM` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): SINGLE_OP 64-bit write and readback of the SPM base word, both SUCCESS, data equal
|`CHK-JTAG2AXI-SMOKE-SERIES-INCR` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): SERIES_CTRL + SERIES_DATA_INCR write then readback at SPM+0x40, both SUCCESS, data equal
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): eight bounded waits (the TAP-state confirmation and seven status polls, the last draining the pipelined series read) each name a finite bound and their last status; none expired
|`CHK-SB-MIN-ACTIVITY` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): the `jtag2axi_req` and `jtag2axi_status` features' comparison counts, from the always-on scoreboard, are at or above the seven bridge transactions the pass launches
|`CHK-SB-JTAG2AXI-REQ`, `CHK-SB-JTAG2AXI-STATUS` |SV-UVM shape only (`--framework uvm`): always-on scoreboard, the embedded DTP's features reused unchanged: every bridge transaction observed by the passive monitor on `smu.dtp_axi_smc_dbg_req/resp` matches the direction, address, size, strobes and strobed data of the request the scan encoded, and every status capture carries the completion's status and, after a read, its data. A required feature with zero comparisons fails the run
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `dtp_under_smu`, `hosted`. Under `--framework uvm` the SV-UVM rows above govern instead, and `+SMU_J2A_SCOREBOARD_NEGATIVE` must fail the run.

'''''

[[sec-061-smu_dtp_scan_chain_boundary_test]]
===== 61 `smu_dtp_scan_chain_boundary_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_dtp_scan_chain_boundary_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` on the primary TAP; TB loops each host scan_out/TDO back to its scan_in/TDI at the wrapper pins; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: The iJTAG SIB hosts and the secondary TAPs leave and re-enter `smu_wrapper` at the pads, and the shift path runs through those boundary pins.

*Test Coverage*:

* With the bench closing each loop at the pins, the leaf gates the three iJTAG hosts by instruction (none selects over a whole IDCODE scan), measures the closed chain length (each payload returns exactly IJTAG_SIB_COUNT bits late for directed and seeded payloads), round-trips the SIB enables, and proves the I/O STAP select at its host pins.
* The loopback is a bench stand-in: it proves the DTP's host-side decode, gating and shift ordering against a zero-length chain, not any pad or adopter chain. It is declared in the *Bench stand-ins and exceptions* section of `SMU_DEFERRED_DISPOSITION.adoc` (Scan-chain loopbacks).
* Expected behaviour comes from the JTAG specification pages, not the RTL. SIB cascade order and count: `hw/ip/jtag/jtag_intf_unit/doc/architecture.adoc` *iJTAG Network* (three cascaded SIBs, listed DFT secure, DFT non-secure, DFD). TDR selection by instruction, SELECT_IJTAG selecting the network, and the falling-edge TDO retimer: `hw/ip/jtag/jtag_ptap/doc/architecture.adoc` *Scan Path*, *iJTAG Support*, *TDO Retiming*. STAP host TMS parked at TMS-Hold when unselected and following the PTAP when selected, and `host_tdo_oen_o` derived from `stap_sel` and the TAP controls: `hw/ip/jtag/jtag_stap/doc/architecture.adoc` *STAP Module*, *3DCR Register*. SEP=1 STAP chain order I/O, SMC debug, SEP debug, extra: `hw/sys/dtp/doc/jtag.adoc` *STAP Secondary Scan Path* and the Integrator Guide's *STAP Scan Chain Topology* with its parameter table (`JTAG_STAP_IO_ENABLE=1`, `JTAG_SMC_DBG_ENABLE=1`, `JTAG_NUM_EXTRA_STAPS=1`). The chain-length expectation is IJTAG_SIB_COUNT: each SIB is one scan cell, the closed loops add none, and IEEE 1149.1 gives a DR of N cells N TCKs of shift latency, the falling-edge retimer being no cell.
* Spec gaps carried as DV-owned rules: the *iJTAG Network* section lists the cascade without stating its TDI-to-TDO direction (the JIU scan-chain figure draws DFT secure nearest TDI; the leaf reads the list top-to-bottom as the STAP table does); no page defines `jtag_scan_ctrl_t.select`, so the host select is expected to follow IEEE 1687 SIB semantics -- asserted only while the network is the active scan path and that SIB is open; the STAP page does not list the TAP states that assert `host_tdo_oen_o`, so the enable is expected for exactly the Shift-IR and Shift-DR TCKs (IEEE 1149.1 Section 4.5.1, with IEEE 1838 running the STAP through both scans).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Run a whole IDCODE IR+DR scan and count the iJTAG host selects on `jtag_tck`; require none.
. Select SELECT_IJTAG and shift five directed and three seeded-random nonzero payloads through the closed chain; require each back IJTAG_SIB_COUNT bits late.
. Update-DR each SIB enable pattern (each alone, all three, none) and read it back on the next Capture-DR; require the matching host select only while that SIB is open.
. Read the unselected I/O STAP quiet, select it through TAP_3DCR, and require its TDO enable to cover exactly IR+DR TCKs with host TMS matching on every TCK while the extra STAP stays quiet.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-IJTAG-GATE` |none of the three iJTAG host selects asserts over a whole IDCODE IR+DR scan, counted on jtag_tck
|`CHK-SMU-IJTAG-CHAIN` |under SELECT_IJTAG the DR closed through the wrapper scan pins returns each payload exactly IJTAG_SIB_COUNT bits late, for five directed and three seeded-random nonzero payloads
|`CHK-SMU-IJTAG-SIB` |Update-DR latches the SIB enables and the next Capture-DR reads them back, for each SIB alone, all three, and none; the matching host select asserts only while that SIB is open
|`CHK-SMU-STAP-IO-SELECT` |an unselected I/O STAP drives no TDO enable and its host TMS does not follow the primary TAP; after a TAP_3DCR select the enable covers exactly IR+DR TCKs, host TMS matches on every TCK, and the extra STAP stays quiet
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `dtp_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''
[[sec-062-smu_otp_bridges_under_dbg_disable_test]]
===== 62 `smu_otp_bridges_under_dbg_disable_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_otp_bridges_under_dbg_disable_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (SMC fabric, SMC OTP and SEP OTP JTAG2AXI bridges); SEP eFuse shadow preload naming a lifecycle state whose posture blocks the fabric bridge; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: The SMC and SEP OTP JTAG2AXI bridges complete while the lifecycle posture has closed the SMC fabric bridge.

*Test Coverage*:

* The posture is read, not assumed: `lc_state` must carry the preloaded state and `dbg_disable.smc_jtag2axi` must be asserted before and after all OTP traffic, so the completions are attributable to the blocked-fabric state.
* A SINGLE_OP on the fabric bridge launches neither AW nor AR on the DTP-to-SMC debug AXI port while that posture holds; the OTP bridges on both subsystems write and read back a spare word with SUCCESS in the same state, the SEP OTP bridge returns the {~raw, raw} LC_STATE word the image programmed, and the SEP shim window's EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME takes and returns a written value.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read `lc_state` and `dbg_disable.smc_jtag2axi` and require the blocking posture.
. Issue a fabric SINGLE_OP and count AW and AR on the DTP-to-SMC debug AXI port; require none.
. Write and read back SMC eFuse MAP SPARE[0] and SEP eFuse MAP SPARE0 over their OTP bridges; read SEP MAP LC_STATE and compare against the image.
. Read, write and read back EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME through the SEP OTP bridge's shim window.
. Re-read `dbg_disable.smc_jtag2axi` and require it still asserted.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-OTP-DBG-POSTURE` |the SEP shadow image puts the chiplet in a lifecycle state whose posture disables dbg_disable.smc_jtag2axi, and lc_state carries that state
|`CHK-OTP-FABRIC-BLOCKED` |a SINGLE_OP on the primary TAP launches neither AW nor AR on the DTP -> SMC debug AXI port while that posture holds
|`CHK-OTP-SMC-RW-WHILE-BLOCKED` |SMC OTP JTAG2AXI write and readback of SMC eFuse MAP SPARE[0] both complete with SUCCESS in the same blocked-fabric state
|`CHK-OTP-SEP-LC-READ-WHILE-BLOCKED` |SEP OTP JTAG2AXI read of SEP eFuse MAP LC_STATE returns the {~raw, raw} word the shadow image programmed
|`CHK-OTP-SEP-RW-WHILE-BLOCKED` |SEP OTP JTAG2AXI write and readback of SEP eFuse MAP SPARE0 both complete with SUCCESS in the same blocked-fabric state
|`CHK-OTP-SEP-BANK-CTRL-READ` |a SEP OTP JTAG2AXI read of the shim window leaves the SEP on the eFuse bank-control port and returns EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME's RDL reset
|`CHK-OTP-SEP-BANK-CTRL-WRITE` |the same port takes a written EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME value and reads it back
|`CHK-OTP-DBG-POSTURE-HELD` |dbg_disable.smc_jtag2axi is still asserted after all OTP traffic, so the completions above are attributable to the blocked-fabric state
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `dtp_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''
[[sec-063-smu_xtrig_ctp_pad_test]]
===== 63 `smu_xtrig_ctp_pad_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_xtrig_ctp_pad_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (SMC fabric JTAG2AXI into the SMC peripheral window that programs the cross-trigger network); every CT_Req_out pad sits on an `ocah_open_drain_bus` shared wire (private, or the group wire the pads in `tb_xtrig_ctp_wire_group` share), resting at the board pull of the port's INVERT sense, with a bench chiplet driver pulling it -- the other three CTP pad groups keep TB drive of the pad; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: The four CTP pad groups of `smu_wrapper` follow the cross-trigger network's mode, handshake and matrix routing at the pads, and a wire-OR port receives cycle-exactly on its shared wire's assertion edge. Every expected level, address and count on the proof path comes from the generated cross-trigger headers or from a DV-owned table transcribed from the cross-trigger specification; the leaf cites no RTL.

*Test Coverage*:

* Register offsets, bitmasks and the CONFIG.MODE reset value come from `hw/ip/cross_trigger/cross_trigger_port/regs/gen/c/` and `hw/ip/cross_trigger/cross_trigger_matrix/regs/gen/c/`; the CTP count, the CTM port count and every per-index CTP and CT_SRC address come from `hw/ip/cross_trigger/cross_trigger_network/regs/gen/c/cross_trigger_network_addr.h`. The pad vectors are required to be `CROSS_TRIGGER_NETWORK_CTP_NUM` wide before any compare.
* The pad-control level per mode is a DV-owned table (`CTP_PAD_LEVELS`) transcribed from the CTP signal interface table in `hw/ip/cross_trigger/cross_trigger_port/doc/interface.adoc`: an enable the table calls "enabled" reads 1, one it calls "disabled" or "enabled by a cross trigger pulse" reads 0 with no trigger in flight, and a data output it calls "static-low" reads 0. That table also pins MODE to 0 (wire-OR) at reset, and the leaf raises if the RDL reset value disagrees. Wire-OR reset and one-lane point-to-point are both checked from the same table, so the P2P leg proves the written lane moved and the other lanes did not.
* The five pad-ring controls checked at 0 (`UNDRIVEN_PAD_CONTROLS`) are the `hw/sys/dtp/doc/port_table.adoc` CTP rows with no counterpart in the CTP signal interface table: CT_Req_in and CT_Ack_in are input pads (CTN architecture, `hw/ip/cross_trigger/cross_trigger_network/doc/architecture.adoc`) and carry no output data or output enable; CT_Ack_out is an output pad and carries no input-buffer enable. Spec gap: no specification sentence states the level these controls are driven to. The DV rule is that a control with no driver reads 0, and the checker is graded against that rule, not against a spec statement.
* Spec gap: the signal interface table calls `ct_ack_out_dout` "unused" in wire-OR mode and describes the two point-to-point data outputs only in terms of the request or acknowledge that drives them. The DV rule reads every data output as 0 while no trigger is in flight.
* The CTM port of the internal lane follows the port-indexing table (external CTPs at 0..N-1, internal lanes after; `hw/ip/cross_trigger/cross_trigger_matrix/doc/architecture.adoc`) and the SMU integrator statement that the low DTP internal-CT lanes are reserved for the SMC and the SMU exposes the lanes above them, with the SMU default of 8 exposed lanes (`doc/integrator/src/smu.adoc`, DTP configuration table; `hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc`). The DTP internal-lane count is the CTM port count less the CTP count from the header, and the `xtrig_ctm_src_req` width is required to equal the exposed count before the port is walked.
* The four-phase handshake runs in both directions at the pads, and a trigger is routed through the cross-trigger matrix to a second port and to an internal cross-trigger lane at `xtrig_ctm_src_req_o`, so the routing is observed at a different pin from the one that sourced it.
* S8 and S9 repeat the point-to-point handshake with matrix routing, and the wire-OR receive, on every one of the `CROSS_TRIGGER_NETWORK_CTP_NUM` lanes, so every lane of every pad group toggles at the pads; S10 routes a trigger into each of the DTP's internal lanes.
* A wire-OR port's `ct_dst` is CT_DST_LATENCY (3 clk_smu: the CTP synchronizer's one 2-FF synchronizer, i.e. two flop stages, plus the registered `ct_dst` output; the same constant as `DTP_CT_DST_LATENCY`) after its shared wire is first seen asserted, never at the release, and never through a reset with the wire resting. A per-clk_smu-cycle activity sampler records the cycle each observable first went high and how many times it rose, so the latency and the one-pulse-per-assertion bound are both cycle-exact.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. With every CT_Req_out wire resting at its pull after reset, observe `tb_xtrig_ctp_ct_dst` and `tb_xtrig_ctp_wire_mismatch` for a window and require no rises and no mismatch.
. Read CONFIG.MODE at reset and every CTP pad control; require the RDL reset value and the wire-OR column of the CTP table on every lane, and 0 on the five undriven controls.
. Write CONFIG.MODE=1 on one lane and require the point-to-point column on that lane, the wire-OR column elsewhere, and 0 on the undriven controls.
. Drive a point-to-point request in, require the acknowledge out and STATUS, release and require it retired.
. Point CT_SRC[1].CT_DST_SELECT at lane 0 and require the trigger on lane 1's request out; route lane 0 into the CT_SRC port of the first SMU-exposed internal lane and require `xtrig_ctm_src_req_o[0]`, then the same from a chiplet pull of lane 2's shared wire, held and released, requiring the receive latency, one pulse, and the routed request following the pull rather than the release.
. Put three wire-OR lanes on the group wire; require one chiplet pull reaches every member's `ct_dst` once at the same latency and leaves every non-member lane quiet, and that two members pulled in overlapping windows still reach every member exactly once and leave every non-member lane quiet, with the mismatch flag clear throughout both windows.
. For every lane in turn, with its successor as the sink, put both in point-to-point, require the pad controls of the point-to-point column on those two lanes alone, route the lane's destination to the sink, drive a request in, require the lane's acknowledge out and the sink's request out, retire it with the sink's acknowledge in, and return the lane to wire-OR.
. With every lane back at wire-OR, pull each lane's private wire in turn and require that lane's `ct_dst` once at CT_DST_LATENCY and no other lane's.
. With lane 0 in point-to-point, route its destination into the CT_SRC port of each DTP internal lane in turn, drive a request, and require that internal lane to rise once and no other, acknowledging it when its mode bit selects the handshake; then pulse each exposed lane's acknowledge input with no request in flight, sampling every cycle of the pulse and of the settle after it, and require no lane to move.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-CTP-DEFAULT` |CONFIG.MODE reads its RDL reset value (wire-OR) and every CTP pad control sits at the wire-OR level of the CTP signal interface table on all `CROSS_TRIGGER_NETWORK_CTP_NUM` lanes
|`CHK-SMU-CTP-TIEOFF` |the five pad-ring controls the CTP signal interface defines no driver for read 0 in both modes (DV rule; see the spec gap above)
|`CHK-SMU-CTP-P2P-MODE` |CONFIG.MODE=1 reads back, moves the written lane's pad controls to the point-to-point level of the CTP signal interface table and leaves every other lane at its wire-OR level
|`CHK-SMU-CTP-P2P-RX` |a point-to-point request in raises the acknowledge out and shows in STATUS, and releasing the request retires it
|`CHK-SMU-CTP-ROUTE` |with CT_SRC[1].CT_DST_SELECT pointing at lane 0, the trigger appears as lane 1's request out and the acknowledge in retires it
|`CHK-SMU-CTM-SRC` |routing lane 0's destination into the CTM port of the first SMU-exposed internal lane raises xtrig_ctm_src_req_o[0], and a chiplet pull of lane 2's shared wire routes the same way, the pulse following the pull rather than the release
|`CHK-SMU-CTP-WIRE-IDLE` |with every CT_Req_out wire resting at its pull after reset, no wire-OR lane's `ct_dst` rises and the wire-mismatch flag is 0
|`CHK-SMU-CTP-WIRE-RX` |a chiplet pull of lane 2's private wire (held >= PULSE_BOUND clk_smu) raises `tb_xtrig_ctp_ct_dst[2]` exactly CT_DST_LATENCY clocks after the wire is first seen asserted, once, and the release raises nothing further
|`CHK-SMU-CTP-WIRE-SHARED` |three wire-OR lanes on one group wire: one chiplet pull reaches every member's `ct_dst` once at the same latency and leaves every non-member lane quiet; two members pulled in overlapping windows still reach every member exactly once and leave every non-member lane quiet
|`CHK-SMU-CTP-EVERY-LANE` |every lane in point-to-point moves its own pad controls, acknowledges a request in, and routes it through the matrix to its successor's request out, which the successor's acknowledge in retires
|`CHK-SMU-CTP-WIRE-RX-EVERY-LANE` |a chiplet pull of each lane's private wire raises that lane's `ct_dst` once at CT_DST_LATENCY and no other lane's
|`CHK-SMU-CTM-EVERY-LANE` |routed into each of the ten DTP internal lanes, the two the SMC keeps and the eight the SMU exposes, a lane-0 request raises that internal lane once and no other, and an acknowledge on an idle exposed lane moves no request on any cycle of the pulse or the settle after it
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `dtp_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''
[[sec-064-smu_xtrig_mode_composition_test]]
===== 64 `smu_xtrig_mode_composition_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_xtrig_mode_composition_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: TB pin drive of the external CTM lanes; `elaboration` firmware mode, `+xtrig_int_ct_mode=0x80`

*Objective*: The per-internal-CT mode vector reaches the DTP, and a handshake lane acknowledges where a pulse-sync lane does not.

*Test Coverage*:

* `doc/integrator/src/smu.adoc` ("Cross Trigger Parameters", "Debug & Test Ports (DTP) Integration") states the lane geometry: 8 SMU-exposed internal CT lanes, 10 at the DTP, the low 2 reserved for the SMC with their mode bits at zero ("SMC-reserved DTP mode bits are 0 (pulse-sync)"). Those counts are the golden of the S1 mask compare and the set of lanes the live leg walks.
* Reading the DTP's mode vector back against the elaborated `CFG` field or the `+xtrig_int_ct_mode` plusarg, the `CFG` decode itself, and the DTP mode vector width are drift checks and carry no evidence token; no specification in this tree states the struct layout.
* What the tokens rest on is the live leg: `port_table.adoc` states that the CTM ack ports are "Unused in pulse-sync mode (mode bit = 0)", and each of the 8 external CTM lanes is requested in turn so that a lane whose mode bit is set acknowledges and holds while a pulse-sync lane does not; the observed acknowledging-lane count must equal the mode popcount.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read the mode vector at the DTP and require the 2 SMC-reserved bits zero.
. Request each of the 8 external CTM lanes in turn and record which acknowledge; require the pulse-sync lanes silent and the handshake lanes to acknowledge and hold.
. Compare the acknowledging-lane count against the mode popcount.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-XTRIG-MODE-S1` |the 2 SMC-reserved mode bits into DTP are zero and a pulse-sync lane never acknowledges
|`CHK-SMU-XTRIG-MODE-S2` |a handshake lane acknowledges and holds; the observed ack-lane count is the mode popcount
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''
==== SEP

[[sec-066-smu_sep_smoke_test]]
===== 66 `smu_sep_smoke_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_smoke_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: TB pin drive only (no external VIP); `sep_smoke` firmware mode -- the SMC runs the `smu_sep_smoke` arm image from ROM and the real SEP RTL boots under it; verdict through `SmuSepBootScoreboard` on the SEP trace probes

*Objective*: The real SEP RTL comes out of reset under the SMU wrapper, fetches from boot ROM, executes from ICCM and stores to DCCM, in the same run in which the SMC arm firmware boots from ROM and signals `TEST_PASS`. The SEP runs on its default run gate; the arm image (`fw/tests/smu_sep_arm/main.c`) writes its marker and does not release the SEP, so no arm-before-boot ordering is claimed.

*Test Coverage*:

* Profile pre-conditions are fail-capable: during cold reset `sep_reset_n_o` must read 0 and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` must read 0 (the real-SEP profile asserts SEP reset and starts with fuse-done inactive); a `no_sep` build fails both.
* Boot readiness is the conjunction the scoreboard's `boot_ready()` requires: SEP reset observed both asserted and released; fuse-done observed low then high *unless* `sep_fuse_sense_skipped_o` is 1, in which case the sense is replaced by the shadow-register preload and the fuse terms are logged as informational rather than counted; SMC arm firmware signalled `TEST_PASS`; boot-ROM fetch seen; ICCM execution seen; at least 256 retired instructions over at least 16 distinct PCs; and at least one DCCM write.
* The fuse-sense carve-out reads the DUT node that *selects* the substitute (`sim_skip_fuse_sense` inside the SEP eFuse shadow registers), not a plusarg scan, so a build without `SIMULATION` keeps the fuse terms required.
* After the SMC arm is seen, 100 000 cycles with `sep_reset_n_o` high and zero retired instructions is a failure with the run-gate state logged, so a released SEP that never fetches is diagnosed rather than timed out.
* Firmware console bytes (`fw_char_o`) are collected and printed; they are not a pass condition.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. In `smu_base_test`, sample `sep_reset_n_o` and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` during cold reset and assert both are 0 for this profile.
. Release reset and, every `clk_smu_i` edge, feed the scoreboard the SEP reset and fuse-done levels, `smc_test_pass_o`, the boot-ROM / ICCM fetch-seen flags, the DCCM write count and any console byte; count retired instructions and distinct PCs from the SEP trace monitor.
. Return as soon as `boot_ready()` holds; fail if 100 000 post-arm cycles pass with no retire, or if the overall cycle budget (`SMU_SEP_BOOT_MAX_CYCLES`, default 3 000 000) expires.
. In `check_phase`, re-assert every readiness term individually so the log names which one was missing.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_profile_precondition` |`sep_reset_n_o == 0` and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o == 0` during cold reset
|`sep_reset_low_high` |SEP reset observed asserted and then released
|`sep_fuse_sense` |fuse-done observed low then high, or `sep_fuse_sense_skipped_o == 1` with the terms demoted to informational
|`smc_arm_pass` |SMC arm firmware wrote `TEST_PASS` (`smc_test_pass_o`)
|`sep_boot_rom_iccm` |boot-ROM fetch seen and ICCM execution seen
|`sep_retire_floor` |>= 256 retired instructions over >= 16 distinct PCs
|`sep_dccm_write` |`sep_dccm_write_count_o` advanced above 0
|`post_arm_idle` |a SEP out of reset retired something within 100 000 cycles of the SMC arm marker being seen
|`CHK-NONVAC` |emitted by the scoreboard only after every term above held
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every readiness term holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_BOOT_ROM_OK`, `SEP_ICCM_OK`, `SEP_DCCM_WRITE_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw_boot`.

'''''

[[sec-067-smu_sep_fuse_sense_done_test]]
===== 67 `smu_sep_fuse_sense_done_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_fuse_sense_done_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: SEP eFuse bank model inside the wrapper composition (declared in *Bench stand-ins and exceptions*); `elaboration` firmware mode under the `sep_rtl_fuse_sense` run mode, which leaves `+skip_fuse_sense` and the SEP shadow preload unset

*Objective*: SEP fuse sense completes on the composition and reports on `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` after the cold reset released and after eFuse read traffic appeared.

*Test Coverage*:

* Measured against the `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` row of `port_table.adoc`; the ordering the checks require comes from the DUT itself and no document in this tree states it, which the card records rather than hides.
* `sep_fuse_sense_skipped_o` -- the bench's view of the SEP shadow registers' own skip flag -- must read 0, so the rising edge timed here is a completed sense and not the plusarg path's forced value.
* eFuse traffic is recognised on the SMU eFuse shim command port by the `valid` bit of `fuse_command_req_t` / `fuse_command_resp_t`, laid out as `hw/ip/efuse/doc/interface.adoc` ("Fuse Command Protocol") gives them; only the ordering is asserted, the sense duration is logged.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Arm the edge and eFuse-command observers ahead of bring-up.
. Require `sep_fuse_sense_skipped_o` low and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` low under cold reset.
. After release, require SEP eFuse read traffic on the shim command port and then exactly one rising edge of `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`, after both.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-FUSE-SENSE-S2` |sep_fuse_sense_done_o rises once, after reset release and after SEP eFuse traffic
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`.

'''''
[[sec-068-smu_sep_secure_tm_test]]
===== 68 `smu_sep_secure_tm_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_secure_tm_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: TB pin drive of `secure_tm_req_i` across three cold resets; `elaboration` firmware mode, `+expected_sep=1`

*Objective*: `secure_tm_req_i` is latched into `secure_tm_o` once per cold reset from the value it holds at the SEP fuse-sense-done edge, not the value it held when the reset asserted, and the strap is ignored between sampling windows.

*Test Coverage*:

* The sampling instant is the specification's: TEST_EN "is latched when SEP fuse sensing is done if SEC_DIS is not asserted" and "on chiplet cold reset release if SEC_DIS is asserted", and the latched value "can be cleared only by chip reset" (`hw/sys/sep/doc/test_mode.adoc`, Test Mode Entry; `hw/sys/sep/doc/lifecycle_controller.adoc`, `secure_tm` and the Secure Scan Chain; `doc/integrator/src/smu.adoc`, the `secure_tm_req_i` bullet). The strap and done ports are the `secure_tm_req_i` and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` rows of `port_table.adoc`.
* In each of three cold resets the strap holds one value as the reset asserts and is changed to the value it is to hold at the edge only after the SEP reset (`obs_sep_rst_n_o`) and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` are both read low; `secure_tm_o` must read low under the reset, stay low across every sample from that change to the edge, and carry the changed value after it. A latch taken when the reset asserts, or a wire, fails these compares. The second reset enters with `secure_tm_o` at 1 from the first, so its low read under reset is an observed 1 -> 0 clear.
* After the edge the strap is changed again and the output must not follow it, in both directions, across a stated number of samples.
* Gap: on the `+skip_fuse_sense` path this leaf runs on (`SMU_DEFERRED_DISPOSITION.adoc`, "`+skip_fuse_sense` with the shadow preload"), `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` rises on the first clock after the SEP reset releases, so the fuse-sense-done edge and cold-reset release are one clock apart and the leaf does not tell the specification's two windows apart. It brackets the sampling instant to after the last strap change under reset and no later than the observed edge. SEC_DIS is not observable at the SMU boundary. Telling the two windows apart needs a run with the real SEP fuse sense, as <<sec-067-smu_sep_fuse_sense_done_test>> runs it, where the edge follows the release by the sense duration.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Drive the strap to one value and assert a cold reset; wait for the SEP reset and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` to read low, require `secure_tm_o` low, then change the strap to the value it is to hold at the edge and keep the reset held.
. Release the reset; require `secure_tm_o` low on every sample until `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` rises and equal to the changed value after it.
. Change the strap with no reset and require `secure_tm_o` unchanged.
. Repeat with the values swapped (1 as the reset asserts, 0 at the edge; the 1 left by the first reset must clear), then once more with the strap held at 1 throughout.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-SECURE-TM` |across three cold resets secure_tm_o clears under the reset, stays low from a strap change made under reset until the SEP fuse-sense-done edge, carries the changed value after the edge rather than the value present when the reset asserted, and ignores the strap between those windows in both directions
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-069-smu_smc_mailbox_int_test]]

* Mailbox 0 and mailbox 31 raise their outbound write-threshold IRQ over the JTAG2AXI frontdoor, and the boundary vector must equal `baseline | 1<<idx` and return to baseline when the enable is cleared.

[[sec-072-smu_smc_smoke_test]]
===== 72 `smu_smc_smoke_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_smoke_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: TB pin drive only (no external VIP); `smc_smoke` firmware mode -- the SMC runs the `smu_smc_smoke` image from ROM under the SEP=1 wrapper build (`compile_smu_chiplet`, the wrapper's one profile); verdict through `SmuSmcBootScoreboard`

*Objective*: SMC firmware boots from ROM and reaches its architectural PASS under the production SMU wrapper, with instruction-memory and scratch activity observed.

*Test Coverage*:

* The profile pre-condition is fail-capable: during cold reset `sep_reset_n_o` must read 0, because the SEP=1 wrapper holds the real SEP reset tree in reset (the same check the elaboration leaf makes); a composition that tied the SEP reset off fails it. `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` after release is logged, not asserted: it is a SEP-side observation this SMC boot claim does not own.
* Firmware boot is one reset-to-completion transaction; reset timing is randomised by `smu_base_test`, and every piece of evidence is a DUT counter or an architectural pass/fail signal, not a TB-side inference.
* Both firmware outcomes are wired: `smc_test_fail_o` asserting fails the run at that cycle; `smc_test_pass_o` ends it.
* Activity floors make a silent PASS impossible: the scoreboard requires `smc_rom_read_count_o > 0` and `smc_scratch_write_count_o > 0` at check time, and the final sample is taken two edges after PASS because the TB write counter updates one edge after the combinational pass flag.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. In `smu_smc_smoke_seq`, assert `sep_reset_n_o == 0` during cold reset and log `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` after release.
. Log the bring-up state (`smc_fuse_sense_done_o`, `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o`, `rst_primary_smc_clk_n_o`, `smc_init_mem_done_o`, `rst_cold_n_o`, `powergood_o`).
. Every `clk_smu_i` edge, sample `smc_test_pass_o`, `smc_test_fail_o`, `smc_rom_read_count_o` and `smc_scratch_write_count_o` into the scoreboard; raise on FAIL.
. On PASS, wait two edges, re-sample the counters, and return; fail on the cycle budget (`SMU_SMC_BOOT_MAX_CYCLES`, default 2 000 000).
. In `check_phase`, require PASS seen, FAIL not seen, and both activity counters above 0.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`sep_reset_precondition` |`sep_reset_n_o == 0` during cold reset on the SEP=1 wrapper
|`smc_test_fail` |`smc_test_fail_o` never asserted (a FAIL ends the run at that cycle)
|`smc_test_pass` |`smc_test_pass_o` asserted before the cycle budget expired
|`smc_rom_read_floor` |`smc_rom_read_count_o > 0`
|`smc_scratch_write_floor` |`smc_scratch_write_count_o > 0`
|`CHK-NONVAC` |emitted by the scoreboard only after every term above held
|===

*Pass Criteria*: PASS observed, FAIL never observed, both activity counters above 0, scoreboard reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SMC_ROM_READ_OK`, `SMC_SCRATCH_WRITE_OK`, `SMC_TEST_PASS_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw_boot`.

'''''

[[sec-075-smu_smc_fuse_sense_sequence_test]]
===== 75 `smu_smc_fuse_sense_sequence_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_fuse_sense_sequence_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: SMC eFuse bank model inside the wrapper composition (declared in *Bench stand-ins and exceptions*); `elaboration` firmware mode under the `sep_rtl_fuse_sense` run mode, which leaves `+skip_fuse_sense` unset

*Objective*: SMC fuse sense completes once after the primary reset released and after eFuse read traffic appeared, and the delayed fuse reset releases after that rise rather than with the cold reset.

*Test Coverage*:

* Measured against the `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` and `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` rows of `port_table.adoc`; the table names the two ports but not their relative order, so the ordering leg is a check on the elaborated design with no document behind it, recorded as such.
* eFuse traffic is recognised on the SMU eFuse shim command port by the `valid` bit of the command structs as `hw/ip/efuse/doc/interface.adoc` ("Fuse Command Protocol") lays them out.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Arm the edge and eFuse-command observers ahead of bring-up.
. After the SMC primary reset releases, require SMC eFuse read traffic and then exactly one rising edge of `smc_fuse_sense_done_o`, after both.
. Require `smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o` to release after that rise and not at the cold-reset release.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-FUSE-SENSE-S1` |smc_fuse_sense_done_o rises once, after reset release and after SMC eFuse traffic
|`CHK-SMU-FUSE-SENSE-S3` |smc_fuse_reset_n_delayed_o releases after that rise, not with the cold reset
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `hosted`.

'''''
[[sec-076-smu_smc_rom_boot_min_pass_test]]
===== 76 `smu_smc_rom_boot_min_pass_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_rom_boot_min_pass_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: the checked-in SMC ROM stub `hw/sys/smc/dv/assets/min_pass.rom.hex` (no toolchain); `ocah_jtag_vip` (SMC fabric JTAG2AXI) for the external readback; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: The SMC CPU fetches and executes the checked-in ROM stub on the wrapper and lands its magic word in CPU_CTRL scratch0, visible to an external master.

*Test Coverage*:

* The scratch0 read is the word the stub stores, so the fetch, the execution and the CSR write all landed; the SMC ROM read counter must have advanced across the boot, so the instruction stream came out of the ROM macro rather than a residual scratch image.
* An external master reading scratch0 at its architectural address over the SMC fabric JTAG2AXI bridge sees the same magic, so the store landed in the CSR and not only in a testbench mirror.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage the ROM stub, bring the SMC up and wait for the scratch0 magic within the boot budget.
. Compare the ROM read counter before and after the boot.
. Read scratch0 over the fabric JTAG2AXI bridge and compare against the magic.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMC-ROM-BOOT-MAGIC` |SMC CPU_CTRL scratch0 reads the word the checked-in ROM stub stores, so the fetch, the execution and the CSR write all landed
|`CHK-SMC-ROM-BOOT-FETCH` |the SMC ROM read counter advanced across the boot, so the instruction stream came out of the ROM macro rather than a residual scratch image
|`CHK-SMC-ROM-BOOT-CSR-VISIBLE` |an external master reading SMC CPU_CTRL scratch0 at its architectural address over the SMC fabric JTAG2AXI bridge sees the same magic, so the store landed in the CSR and not only in a testbench mirror
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `smc_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''
[[sec-077-smu_smc_wdt_boundary_timeout_test]]
===== 77 `smu_smc_wdt_boundary_timeout_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_wdt_boundary_timeout_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (SMC fabric JTAG2AXI for the watchdog and CPU_CTRL registers); `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: The CORE0 watchdog's first and second timeouts reach the SMU pins, the watchdog timeout drops the SMC warm reset, and the warm reset clears the first timeout.

*Test Coverage*:

* <<sec-074-smu_smc_wdt_timeout_irq_test>> stops at the watchdog's pending bit because it never sets WDOGRSTEN, which "enables a sticky reset signal to outputted to top level" (`wdt.rdl`); this leaf sets it and follows the timeout out to `smc_wdt_first_timeout_o`, with WDOGIP0 set at the same time and both clear beforehand.
* The held first timeout runs the CPU_CTRL.WDT_TIMEOUT count (`cpu_ctrl.rdl`: "Cycle count value that 2nd stage watchdog timer timeout should occur at") out to `smc_wdt_second_timeout_o`. A watchdog timeout is a warm reset source (`hw/sys/smc/doc/clk_rst.adoc`, "Watchdog Timeout"; `hw/sys/smc/doc/cpu.adoc`: "a Warm Reset from a watchdog timeout"), so the SMC warm reset must drop; the cycles of the second timeout and of the drop are recorded.
* The warm reset covers the per-core watchdog timers (`clk_rst.adoc`, "Warm Reset"), so the first timeout must fall. The levels the second timeout, the SMC warm reset and the SMC watchdog reset settle at, and whether the watchdog reset asserted, are recorded.
* Spec gap: the SMC chapters do not state which watchdog stage asserts the warm reset, what the second timeout does after the warm reset, or whether the warm reset releases without the cold reset that `cpu.adoc` says follows a watchdog timeout; those are observations.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read both timeout pins and WDOGIP0 clear; arm CORE0 with WDOGRSTEN, WDOGENALWAYS and WDOGZEROCMP.
. Wait for `smc_wdt_first_timeout_o` and read WDOGIP0 set at the same time.
. Hold the first timeout, wait for `smc_wdt_second_timeout_o` and for the SMC warm reset to drop, recording both cycles.
. Wait for the first timeout to fall, and record the second timeout and both SMC resets.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-WDT-FIRST` |CORE0 armed with WDOGRSTEN as well as WDOGENALWAYS and WDOGZEROCMP raises smc_wdt_first_timeout_o, with WDOGIP0 set at the same time and both clear beforehand
|`CHK-SMU-WDT-SECOND` |the held first timeout runs the CPU_CTRL.WDT_TIMEOUT counter out to smc_wdt_second_timeout_o, and the SMC warm reset, released before, drops within the same window
|`CHK-SMU-WDT-RELEASE` |after the warm reset, smc_wdt_first_timeout_o falls
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `smc_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''
==== SMU_WRAPPER

`--dut smu` is the wrapper bench, and `testlists/all.toml` enrols the whole
SMU regression on it. The entries in this section are the wrapper-native
names: the elaboration leaf, the probe leaf and the SEP firmware set.

Every entry in this section was derived from the test body and its sequence
under `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/`, not from the testlist name. Where a sequence's
own docstring records what a leaf does *not* prove, that wording is carried
into Test Coverage rather than dropped.

Two things are worth reading before the entries:

* *The verdict is usually the firmware's.* Most of these boot a real SEP DV
  image that parks in a named terminal loop, and the run is classified by which
  loop PC the SEP settles on. `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/sep_terminal_loop_seq.py`
  resolves those loop addresses from the staged `.sym`, refuses a run whose
  ITCM image and symbol table come from different firmwares, and reports a fail
  loop the linker garbage-collected as a path *not covered* rather than as
  passed. Its tokens are `+++CHK-<NAME>-FRONTDOOR+++` and
  `+++CHK-<NAME>-VERDICT+++`, where `<NAME>` is the sequence's own `NAME`
  upper-cased, so the underscores in e.g. `CHK-SEP_AES-VERDICT` are deliberate.
* *`smu_sep_smoke_test` and `smu_smc_smoke_test` are not listed again here.*
  <<sec-066-smu_sep_smoke_test>> and <<sec-072-smu_smc_smoke_test>> describe the
  `cocotb_wrapper/tests/` bodies those names enrol, which are the only bodies of
  those names in the tree.

[[sec-078-smu_wrapper_elaboration_test]]
===== 78 `smu_wrapper_elaboration_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_wrapper_elaboration_test`

*BFM / Model*: TB pin drive only (no external VIP); `elaboration` firmware mode, so no CPU image is staged

*Objective*: SMU_ALL_001: the SEP=1 wrapper composes SMC+SEP+DTP+xbar on one clock/reset domain and exports the SEP lifecycle state.

*Test Coverage*:

* `+expected_sep=1` selects `_run_smu_all_001`, the DV-CARD SMU_ALL_001 path; this is the only leaf that runs it.
* The S1 setup is a driven observation, not a sampled level: the sequence deasserts `powergood_i` and requires `obs_powergood_stable_o` to fall after a proven high and to rise again afterwards, so a composition that never sees power-good deasserted fails the bounded wait.
* Hierarchical clock identity is sampled at the SMC, SEP, DTP and xbar levels and each must equal the top-level `clk_smu` level on the same edge.
* The three clock domains (`clk_smu`, telemetry/reference, SEP watchdog) must have distinct periods, and their level transitions counted over a fixed 240 ns window must differ accordingly -- a coincidence is ruled out by the period check, not assumed.
* `lc_state_o` at the SMU boundary is compared against the SEP-sourced lifecycle value, so the export is a compare and not a tie-off read.
* S3 is scoped: `port_group=smu_axi,lifecycle` is proven; `jtag,xtrig` is logged explicitly as `NOT-PROVEN` because this step drives neither.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Drive a `powergood_i` deassertion and observe `obs_powergood_stable_o` go 1 -> 0 -> 1 around it, then wait for `rst_cold_n_o` to release.
. Assert `sep_enabled_o == 1` -- this leaf refuses to run against a `no_sep` build.
. Sample the SMC/SEP/DTP/xbar hierarchical clock levels against the top-level clock on the same edges.
. Observe the primary SMC reset through a cold assert and a cold deassert.
. Count level transitions on three DUT clock nodes over 240 ns and compare them against the configured periods, which must themselves be distinct.
. Read the SEP lifecycle value and compare `lc_state_o` against it.
. Check the timeout-path inventory carries a finite bound and a last-state diagnostic, then run the non-vacuity fence.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-COMPOSE-BLOCKS-S1` |`sep=1`, blocks `smc+sep+dtp+xbar` present, hierarchical clock identity holds at every sampled level
|`CHK-SMU-COMPOSE-BLOCKS-S2` |`clk_smu` domain with `rst_primary_smc_clk_no`: last sampled clock levels and the primary reset agree
|`CHK-SMU-COMPOSE-BLOCKS-S3` |`port_group=smu_axi,lifecycle` proven; `port_group=jtag,xtrig` is logged `NOT-PROVEN` in the same step
|`CHK-SMU-PORT-CLK-RST-S1` |cold assert and cold deassert both observed on `rst_primary_smc`
|`CHK-SMU-PORT-CLK-RST-S3` |telemetry and SEP-watchdog clocks are separate from `clk_smu_i`: distinct periods and distinct 240 ns transition counts
|`CHK-SMU-SEP-PARAM-S1` |`sep=1`, xbar present, and `lc_state_o` matches the SEP-sourced lifecycle value
|`CHK-TIMEOUT-PATHS` |the S8 timeout inventory carries a finite bound and a last-state diagnostic on every path
|`CHK-NONVAC` |step ordering, simulation-time advance and the DUT comparison floor all hold
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `fw_boot`, `elaboration`.

'''''
[[sec-079-smu_wrapper_jtag_ptap_test]]
===== 79 `smu_wrapper_jtag_ptap_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_wrapper_jtag_ptap_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (`OcahJtagMasterDriver` on the primary TAP); the SEP runs the `hello_world` DV image

*Objective*: The primary TAP answers on the production wrapper while the SEP is executing, and TAP access does not disturb it.

*Test Coverage*:

* S1 IDCODE matches the RTL default and TDO is resolved, so the TAP answers rather than reading back as an unresolved 0.
* S2 BYPASS is a one-bit register: over an 8-bit scan TDO returns the pattern shifted up one bit. IDCODE alone can be satisfied by a stuck chain; the one-bit delay cannot.
* S3 both results hold at a second TCK period, so neither is tied to one clock ratio against `clk_smu`.
* S4 the SEP retire count still advances across the TAP accesses -- the part specific to this profile.
* S5 the IC_RESET override bits are clear, which every other wrapper leaf rests on: an asserted override holds SMC cold/fuse reset and the SEP never fetches.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Build the primary TAP through `wrapper_jtag` and read IDCODE, checking the value and that TDO is resolved.
. Shift an 8-bit pattern through BYPASS and compare TDO against the pattern delayed by exactly one bit.
. Repeat both at a second TCK period.
. Sample the SEP retire count before and after the TAP accesses and require it to have advanced.
. Read back the IC_RESET override TDR and require the SMC and SEP override bits to be clear.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-WRAPPER-PTAP-IDCODE` |IDCODE read at the sampled TCK period matches the RTL default and TDO is resolved
|`CHK-WRAPPER-PTAP-BYPASS` |one-bit register at each TCK period; a stuck chain cannot produce the TDI delay
|`CHK-WRAPPER-PTAP-SEP-LIVE` |the SEP retire count advanced across the TAP accesses
|`CHK-WRAPPER-IC-RESET-CLEAR` |SMC override bits and the SEP override read back clear, so the base test's TAP reset walk really does clear the TDR
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_probe`.

'''''
[[sec-080-smu_sep_boot_health_test]]
===== 80 `smu_sep_boot_health_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_boot_health_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_boot_health` (on-chip verdict), classified through the SEP trace probes

*Objective*: The SEP boots the real DV boot-health image and parks in its own pass loop, with the boot order observed.

*Test Coverage*:

* Runs the same image the internal SMU suite boots, not a minimal freestanding smoke.
* The verdict is the firmware's: it parks in one of two named terminal loops and the run is classified by which loop PC the SEP settles on.
* Loop addresses come from the staged `.sym`, so the contract survives a firmware relink.
* `boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` first-seen ordering is asserted, so a pass loop reached without a real boot fails.
* SEP-local cold scratch7 (`0x1080_2038`) marker observation is out of scope here.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Load the staged symbol table and resolve the pass and fail loop addresses; a pass loop outside the ICCM window is a failure.
. Run the SEP and sample `sep_trace_valid_o` / `sep_pc_o` each `clk_smu` edge, recording first boot-ROM fetch, first ICCM execution and first pass-loop PC.
. Stop at the first terminal loop entered and classify the run by that PC.
. Assert the boot-ROM, ICCM and ordering conditions, then emit the evidence tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-BOOT-HEALTH-FRONTDOOR` |first retired PC is the boot-ROM reset vector, and both boot-ROM and ICCM execution were seen
|`CHK-SEP-BOOT-HEALTH-VERDICT` |the pass loop was reached and no fail loop was entered
|`CHK-NONVAC` |`boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` first-seen ordering holds
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_REAL_FW_BOOT_OK`, `SEP_REAL_FW_PASS_LOOP_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-081-smu_sep_rom_tcm_load_test]]
===== 81 `smu_sep_rom_tcm_load_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_rom_tcm_load_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/rom_no_tcm_preload_mem_init` running from boot ROM with `+sep_no_tcm_preload`; verdict through the SEP trace probes and the STDOUT mailbox

*Objective*: The SEP loads its own ICCM from boot ROM and executes it, with no testbench TCM preload.

*Test Coverage*:

* Every other real-firmware anchor here has its ICCM/DCCM placed by the testbench backdoor; that backdoor hides the CPU loading its own TCM. This leaf is the one that closes the gap.
* `+sep_no_tcm_preload` leaves the TCM holding zeroes when the CPU starts, so ICCM execution is only reachable if the firmware's own DMA put code there.
* The image writes and reads back DCCM from ROM-fetched code, stages a routine into SEP SRAM as raw instruction words, opens the secure-DMA range, disables local-alias remap, DMAs SRAM to ICCM, and jumps to the copy.
* The ICCM PC is checked separately from the mailbox verdict: reaching the DMA target is what distinguishes a real load from a firmware that reported PASS some other way.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Run with `+sep_no_tcm_preload` so no ICCM/DCCM image is backdoored.
. Observe boot-ROM execution, then the DMA-done marker, then a retired PC inside the ICCM window.
. Read the STDOUT mailbox for `TEST_MAGIC_PASS`.
. Assert all three in order before emitting the evidence tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-ROM-TCM-LOAD` |ROM execution, then `dma_done`, then ICCM execution at the DMA target, then `TEST_MAGIC_PASS`; the TCM was zero at reset
|`CHK-SEP-ROM-TCM-NONVAC` |no TB TCM preload was applied, so ICCM execution is only possible because the firmware's own DMA put code there
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_ROM_TCM_LOAD_OK`, `SEP_SELF_LOADED_ICCM_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-082-smu_sep_sanity_test]]
===== 82 `smu_sep_sanity_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_sanity_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_sanity` (HMAC + KMAC KAT, on-chip verdict); STDOUT mailbox handshake at `0x8000_0000`

*Objective*: The SEP runs SHA-256 (HMAC) and SHA3-256 (KMAC) known-answer vectors on-chip and reports PASS through the mailbox.

*Test Coverage*:

* The firmware programs its own SEP outbound filter, runs both KATs, then reports `TEST_MAGIC0 -> TEST_MAGIC_PASS` per `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/include/tb.h`.
* The verdict is the firmware's, not the testbench's: a PASS means the SEP compared both digests against the NIST vectors on-chip. The TB only observes.
* Five `STAGE_BEACON` words (`0xB1B0B0B_<id>`) give stage-level progress -- main entered, filter programmed, HMAC entered, HMAC done / KMAC entered, KMAC done -- and all five are required.
* Beacon 0 follows `sep_outbound_filter_init()`: a store ahead of it faults against the `BLOCK_BY_DEFAULT=1` outbound filter and kills the image, so the beacon's absence is a real failure, not a missing print.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Boot the image and watch the SEP trace for ICCM execution.
. Collect the five stage beacons in order as the firmware publishes them.
. Read the STDOUT mailbox and require `TEST_MAGIC_PASS`.
. Assert the `iccm exec < beacon 1..4 < PASS magic` ordering before emitting the tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-SANITY-STAGES` |all five stage beacons were reached in the SEP: the filter, HMAC and KMAC stages were each entered and left
|`CHK-SEP-SANITY-KAT` |the firmware wrote `TEST_MAGIC_PASS` -- both known-answer vectors matched on-chip
|`CHK-NONVAC` |`iccm exec < beacon 1..4 < PASS magic` ordering holds
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_REAL_FW_SANITY_OK`, `SEP_HMAC_KMAC_KAT_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-083-smu_sep_modules_test]]
===== 83 `smu_sep_modules_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_modules_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_modules` (on-chip verdict); OCAH-local ESRC noise driver under `+esrc_noise_force`

*Objective*: The SEP module matrix -- AES, HMAC and KMAC in this build -- clears on-chip, with the failing module named.

*Test Coverage*:

* The image walks the SEP module touch-points its stage mask enables and parks in a per-stage terminal loop, so a failure names the module rather than reporting "firmware did not pass".
* The stage mask is compile-time and the linker garbage-collects loops for disabled stages, so the watch set is derived from the symbol table: a stage that is not built has no loop symbol, and a stage that is built must have one.
* `+esrc_noise_force` supplies raw noise the ring oscillators cannot generate under Verilator.
* This is a matrix over module reachability, not a cryptographic depth test; the dedicated AES vector is `smu_sep_aes_test`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Start the ESRC noise driver.
. Resolve the per-stage pass and fail loop symbols from the staged `.sym`, treating an absent symbol as a stage not covered rather than a stage passed.
. Run the SEP and classify by the terminal loop PC reached.
. Assert the boot-ROM / ICCM / ordering conditions and emit the tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-MODULES-FRONTDOOR` |first retired PC is the boot-ROM reset vector, boot-ROM and ICCM execution both seen
|`CHK-SEP-MODULES-MATRIX` |every enabled stage cleared on-chip; the pass loop was reached and no per-stage fail loop was entered
|`CHK-NONVAC` |`boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` ordering holds
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_REAL_FW_MODULES_OK`, `SEP_MODULE_MATRIX_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-084-smu_sep_aes_test]]
===== 84 `smu_sep_aes_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_aes_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_aes` (on-chip KAT verdict); OCAH-local ESRC noise driver under `+esrc_noise_force`

*Objective*: One AES-128 ECB known-answer vector is encrypted and compared on-chip, with the alert status checked before the image parks.

*Test Coverage*:

* The same AES-128 ECB known-answer vector the AES stage of `smu_sep_modules_test` runs -- `sep_smu_aes` and `sep_smu_modules` share the key, plaintext and expected ciphertext -- on a dedicated image whose only subject is AES. What this leaf adds is the cipher in isolation from the HMAC and KMAC stages and the alert-status check before the image parks, not a second vector.
* AES masking reseeds its PRNG from crypto-EDN, so the image brings the entropy stack up first and parks in a *distinct* fail loop if that does not complete -- the prerequisite and the cipher stay separately attributable.
* The sequence refuses to run without `+esrc_noise_force`: without driven noise the image stalls at the ESRC boot gate and never reaches the cipher, which would otherwise look like an AES failure.
* The cycle budget clears the ESRC boot health-test window (2048 samples at div64) with margin; the KAT itself is a few thousand cycles.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Assert `+esrc_noise_force` was passed, then start the ESRC noise driver.
. Resolve `smu_sep_aes_pass_loop`, `smu_sep_aes_fail_loop` and `smu_sep_aes_fail_entropy_loop` from the staged `.sym`.
. Run the SEP and classify by the terminal loop PC reached.
. Assert the front-door and ordering conditions and emit the tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`+CHK-SEP_AES-FRONTDOOR+` |first retired PC is the boot-ROM reset vector, boot-ROM and ICCM execution both seen
|`+CHK-SEP_AES-VERDICT+` |the pass loop was reached and neither the AES nor the entropy fail loop was entered
|`CHK-NONVAC` |`boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` ordering holds
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_REAL_FW_AES_OK`, `SEP_AES_ECB128_KAT_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-085-smu_sep_otbn_test]]
===== 85 `smu_sep_otbn_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_otbn_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_otbn`, classified through the SEP trace probes

*Objective*: The OTBN register aperture is reachable over the SEP-internal fabric -- a reachability claim, not an OTBN functional one.

*Test Coverage*:

* The image writes INTR_ENABLE, INTR_STATE, ERR_BITS, INSN_CNT and LOAD_CHECKSUM, reads INTR_ENABLE back, and parks in its pass loop only when the readback equals the value written.
* OTBN is at `0x1090_0000`, inside the SEP's own peripheral region, so these accesses travel the SEP-internal fabric. The outbound filter is not on this path: its only open window is the 256-byte STDOUT mailbox, and a write behind it would have been blocked rather than completed.
* *What this proves*: reaching the pass loop means no access took a store fault -- `crt0` installs a trap handler that parks in `_finish` instead -- and that the INTR_ENABLE write arrived and stuck, because the image compares its own readback and branches to the fail loop on a mismatch. That closes the default-slave case, where a write is answered without reaching the register.
* *What it does not prove*: nothing about OTBN functionality and nothing about the SEP outbound fabric. There is no IMEM/DMEM load and no EXECUTE; the other four writes are covered only by the absence of a fault; no transaction is counted at the OTBN aperture itself.
* This image needs no entropy -- it touches neither AES masking nor the Key Manager.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Resolve the pass and fail loop symbols from the staged `.sym`.
. Run the SEP and classify by the terminal loop PC reached.
. Assert the front-door and ordering conditions and emit the token.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`+CHK-SEP_OTBN-FRONTDOOR+` |first retired PC is the boot-ROM reset vector, boot-ROM and ICCM execution both seen
|`+CHK-SEP_OTBN-VERDICT+` |the pass loop was reached -- i.e. no CSR access faulted and the firmware's own INTR_ENABLE readback matched -- and no fail loop was entered
|`CHK-NONVAC` |`boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` ordering holds
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_REAL_FW_OTBN_CSR_REACHABLE`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-086-smu_sep_entropy_test]]
===== 86 `smu_sep_entropy_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_entropy_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_entropy_bringup`; OCAH-local ESRC noise driver under `+esrc_noise_force`

*Objective*: The SEP firmware brings up ESRC -> CSRNG -> EDN and entropy is proven to flow, not merely to have been programmed.

*Test Coverage*:

* The image programs the stack through the driver the SEP DV tree already ships, in the order that driver documents.
* Programming the stack is *not* the claim: a firmware that wrote the registers into a dead block would reach its pass loop just the same.
* The claim is checked at four points down the chain -- `esrc_noise_active` (lane-0's `dcor.noise_i` tracks the driven bit, so the force took), `drbg_seed_valid` (ESRC produced a seed the health tests accepted), `drbg_es_ack` (CSRNG consumed it) and `drbg_genbits_vld` (the CTR_DRBG produced genbits).
* Bit-exact prediction of the genbits is not attempted; that needs the golden chain in `hw/sys/sep/dv` and belongs there.
* This is the anchor the AES and OTBN entropy consumers were waiting on.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Start the ESRC noise driver and confirm lane-0's `dcor.noise_i` tracks the driven bit.
. Run the firmware bring-up and observe the PHASE-A / generators / PHASE-B programming order.
. Observe `drbg_seed_valid`, then `drbg_es_ack`, then `drbg_genbits_vld`.
. Require the firmware to reach its pass loop, then emit the tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-ENTROPY-FW-BRINGUP` |the firmware programmed ESRC/CSRNG/EDN in the documented PHASE-A / generators / PHASE-B order and reached its pass loop
|`CHK-SEP-ENTROPY-FLOWS` |forced noise reached `dcor.noise_i`, ESRC produced an accepted seed, CSRNG consumed it and the CTR_DRBG produced genbits -- the chain runs, not just its registers
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_ENTROPY_FW_BRINGUP_OK`, `SEP_ENTROPY_CHAIN_FLOWS_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_entropy`.

'''''
[[sec-087-smu_sep_efuse_test]]
===== 87 `smu_sep_efuse_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_efuse_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_efuse` (on-chip verdict), classified by the terminal-loop PC the SEP settles on; loop addresses come from the staged `.sym`, so the contract survives a relink

*Objective*: The SEP-side eFuse control and external-shim CSR read/write path clears on-chip.

*Test Coverage*:

* The firmware exercises the SEP eFuse control and external-shim CSR path and parks in its own pass or fail loop.
* The verdict is the firmware's; the testbench only watches which loop the SEP settles on.
* This is the SEP-side eFuse CSR path. It carries no claim about SMC eFuse, OTP burn or lifecycle decode -- those are `smu_sep_lcc_*`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Require the staged ITCM image and the `.sym` to name the same firmware -- sibling images share a crt0 layout, so loop addresses collide and a mismatched pair would give a confident, wrong PASS.
. Resolve the pass loop and the fail loops (`efuse`) from the symbol table; a fail symbol the linker dropped is reported as a path not covered, not as passed.
. Run the SEP, sampling `sep_trace_valid_o` / `sep_pc_o` each `clk_smu` edge, and stop at the first terminal loop entered.
. Assert the boot-ROM, ICCM and `boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` ordering conditions, then emit the evidence tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`+CHK-SEP_SEP_EFUSE-FRONTDOOR+` |first retired PC is the boot-ROM reset vector, and both boot-ROM and ICCM execution were seen
|`+CHK-SEP_SEP_EFUSE-VERDICT+` |the pass loop was reached and no fail loop was entered
|`CHK-NONVAC` |`boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` ordering holds
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_REAL_FW_EFUSE_OK`, `SEP_EFUSE_CSR_PATH_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
