[[sec-090-smu_sep_spi_test]]
===== 90 `smu_sep_spi_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_spi_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`), sequence `cocotb_wrapper/seq_lib/smu_sep_spi_seq.py`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_spi` (the transfer and its on-chip stage checks), classified by the terminal-loop PC the SEP settles on; a per-`clk_smu`-cycle pad monitor on the SMC pad ports of `smu.sv`; the DV-owned pin table `SPI_PIN_TABLE` in the sequence as the reference, each row cited to a spec file and line; a bench SPI device that drives a seeded word on the receive pin through `tb_gpio_drive_en` / `tb_gpio_drive_val`, with the stream from `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/env/sep_seeded_rng.py`

*Objective*: While the SEP firmware runs a real command / address / receive transfer, the SMC SPI pins carry the SEP OpenTitan SPI host request as the pin table states on every cycle; the transfer has the SCK count, CS# framing, output enables and transmit bytes the firmware asks for; a word driven on the receive pin reaches RXDATA; and the pins stay still while the host is idle.

*Test Coverage*:

* Probe point. The pad side is read on the `smu.sv` ports `core2pad_o`, `core2pad_en_o` and `pad2core_i`, the deepest point the open tree owns. The host side is read on the ports of `u_sep_ot_spi_wrap` (`spi_sck_o`, `spi_sck_oe_o`, `spi_cs_no`, `spi_cs_oe_o`, `spi_sd_o`, `spi_sd_oe_o`, `spi_sd_i`), upstream of `smu.sv`, and the RX FIFO read port of `u_spi_host` (`rx_valid`, `rx_ready`, `rx_data`). Every probe is resolved by its full path before the run; a path that does not resolve fails the leaf, and there is no second path. `hw/sys/smu/dv/tb/smu_wrapper_public_scope.vlt` makes those host signals visible on Verilator.
* Reference. The pin table is DV-owned and cites only spec files, never the RTL that implements the pins. A spec-vs-RTL disagreement on a graded row fails the leaf. The sources: GPIO = `doc/integrator/meta/ocah_gpio_table.csv`; SEP = `hw/sys/sep/doc/spi.adoc`; OT = `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/spi_host/data/spi_host.hjson`; OTDOC = the OpenTitan SPI Host documentation that `hw/sys/sep/doc/spi.adoc:69-71` names authoritative; EN = `doc/starting/src/guidelines.adoc:166-167` (an active-low name carries `_n`) and `hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc:105-108` (`core2pad_en_o` is the core-to-pad path enable, so active high). The host enables are the OpenTitan output-buffer enables that CONTROL.OUTPUT_EN gates (OT :221-225).
+
[cols="1,2,2,2,2,3",options="header"]
|===
|GPIO |Function |`core2pad_o` carries |`core2pad_en_o` follows |`pad2core_i` feeds |Source
|0-3 |SPI.DATA[n] |host `sd[n]` |host `sd_en[n]` |host `sd_i[n]` |GPIO :2-5; SEP :48 (4-bit bidirectional I/O)
|4-7 |SPI.DATA[n] |not graded |held 0 (no SEP source) |- |GPIO :6-9; SEP :35-36 (four data lines)
|8 |SPI.CS |host `csb` |host `csb_en` |- |GPIO :10; OT :113-116 (csb, active low)
|9 |SPI.CLK |host `sck` |host `sck_en` |- |GPIO :11; OT :110-112
|10 |SPI.DQS |not graded |held 0 (no SEP source) |- |GPIO :12; SEP :37-38 (no DQS)
|54 |SPI DQS Loopback |not graded |held 0 (no SEP source) |- |GPIO :56; SEP :37-38
|===
+
In standard mode SD[0] carries host-to-device data and SD[1] device-to-host data (OTDOC), so the transmit pin is GPIO 0, the receive pin is GPIO 1, and lanes 2-3 are unused.
* Spec gaps, for an owner decision. Graded with no spec text: the SMC GPIO `SPI.*` functions are driven by the SEP SPI host (the GPIO table names the function `SPI` and no document names its source); host lane _n_ is `SPI.DATA[n]` (by name only); a pin with no SEP source is not output-enabled (DV rule); the standard-mode lane roles come from OTDOC, which is not in the repository. Not graded, because no spec states them: the SPI function select (`lsio_interface_select_o`), the pad input enable (`pad2core_en_o`) of every SPI pin, the pad data level on pins with no SEP source, and whether a data lane may be driven while CS# is high. The leaf logs the table and these gaps on every run.
* Stimulus. The firmware runs three standard-speed segments with CONFIGOPTS.CPOL=0: a 1-byte TX (0x9F, pushed as one byte with a byte store; TXDATA takes byte enables, OT :518), a 4-byte TX (one word, 0x03 and the address 0x001000, CSAAT=1) and a 4-byte RX (CSAAT=0). A segment moves LEN+1 bytes (OT :443-453), CSAAT=0 raises CS# at its end (OT :470-476), and with CPOL=0 SCK idles low and emits one high pulse per bit (OT :352-356): 8 x (1 + 4 + 4) = 72 SCK rises in two CS# windows of 8 and 64 rises. The sequence holds the byte counts as `FW_SEGMENTS` and the pushed bytes as `FW_TXDATA_BYTES`; the firmware header points back to them.
* Response stimulus. The bench device drives a 32-bit word drawn from the seed (`RANDOM_SEED`, salted) on the receive pin, one bit per receive SCK period. With CPHA=0 the data changes on the trailing edge and is sampled on the leading edge (OT :360-364), so the device changes the bit on the SCK falling edge; it releases the pin after the last receive bit and when CS# rises. The draw is rejected until the four bytes are distinct and none is 0x00 or 0xFF, so a stuck lane, a lane swap or a byte-order fault gives a different word. The word is a function of the seed only; it is not evidence on its own.
* Firmware verdict. The image checks on-chip that each stage reaches READY / idle, that ERROR_STATUS has no CMDINVAL / CSIDINVAL, that exactly one word lands in the RX FIFO and that the FIFO then drains. It parks in one of eight per-stage fail loops, or in a catch-all loop the compiler can drop (then reported as a path not covered). The image does not judge the received word: the bench grades it.
* Graded window. Every `clk_smu` cycle from the first cycle the monitor samples with the SEP's ICCM-fetch latch (`sep_iccm_fetch_seen_o`) set to 2000 cycles after the firmware verdict. The window starts before the firmware enables the host outputs, which the OE checker proves with at least one cycle of released SCK and CS# pins.
* Controls. The host SCK output is counted on the same window as the pad SCK, and both must equal 72. Each host field that a pin follows (`sck`, `sck_en`, `csb`, `csb_en`, data lane 0, its enable) must take both values in the window, so the map checker grades both polarities. The idle checker needs a pre-transfer window of at least 200 cycles and a post-transfer window of at least 2000 cycles with no pad change, and it needs SCK, CS# and the transmit pin to move as data and as output enable inside the transfer.
* X/Z. Graded on VCS, not run on Verilator. On a four-state simulator (`run_dv.py --dut smu --tool vcs`) `CHK-SEP-SPI-PAD-XZ` requires every probed pad bit, host net and RX FIFO handshake bit to read 0 or 1 on every cycle of the graded window. Verilator is two-state, so there the check cannot fail and is not run: the leaf logs that the four-state X/Z check is not run and emits no X/Z evidence. On either simulator a sample the monitor cannot read as 0/1 fails the run, because the checkers cannot grade that cycle.
* Out of scope. Excluded for OSS hygiene: proprietary IPs in nonfree. Dual and quad speed and lanes 1-3 as outputs are not run, so the map checker grades the data and enable of GPIO 1-3 at the single value the host gives in standard mode.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Resolve every pad, host and RX FIFO probe and the bench drive pins by full path; require each pad vector to be wide enough for GPIO 54.
. Draw the receive word from the seed; log it with the expected SCK count, the rises per CS# window and the TXDATA bytes, then log the pin table and the spec gaps.
. Start the pad monitor, then run the base terminal-loop flow: require the staged ITCM image and the `.sym` to name the same firmware, resolve the pass loop and the fail loops, run the SEP until it parks, and require the boot-ROM, ICCM and `boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` ordering.
. On every cycle of the graded window: sample every probe, compare each pin of the table with the host, count host and pad SCK rises and CS# falls, record the enables and the transmit pin at each pad SCK rise, record each RX FIFO pop, and run the bench device.
. Keep sampling for 2000 cycles after the verdict, then grade the checkers below and emit the pad evidence tokens only when all of them hold.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`+CHK-SEP_SPI-FRONTDOOR+` |the first retired PC is the boot-ROM reset vector, and the SEP executes from both the boot ROM and the ICCM. Fails when the SEP does not boot from its ROM or never runs the image
|`+CHK-SEP_SPI-VERDICT+` |the SEP parks in the pass loop and enters no fail loop. Fails when a stage of the transfer stalls, ERROR_STATUS flags the command or the chip select, or the RX FIFO does not hold exactly one word and then drain
|`CHK-NONVAC` |`boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` ordering holds
|`CHK-SEP-SPI-PAD-MAP` |on every cycle of the window, each graded cell of the pin table holds: `core2pad_o` carries the host output, `core2pad_en_o` follows the host enable or is 0 on a pin with no SEP source, and the host data input of each lane equals `pad2core_i` of its pin; each host field the pins follow took both values. Fails when a pin is routed to the wrong GPIO, inverted, tied, or not enabled as the table states, or the wrong pad feeds a host input
|`CHK-SEP-SPI-PAD-SCK` |the SCK pin (GPIO 9) rises exactly 72 times, the count the firmware's byte counts give, and the host `spi_sck_o` rises 72 times on the same window. Fails when SCK does not reach the pin, toggles extra, or the host clocks a different number of bits
|`CHK-SEP-SPI-PAD-FRAME` |the CS# pin (GPIO 8) falls twice, once per firmware CS# window, and the host CS# also falls twice; the windows hold 8 and 64 SCK rises; no SCK rise occurs with CS# high; CS# is high again at the end. Fails when CS# does not reach the pin, is stuck, or frames the bits differently from CSAAT
|`CHK-SEP-SPI-PAD-OE` |the enables are graded at all 72 pad SCK rises (fewer rises fail the checker); at every one the SCK and CS# pins are driven, the receive pin (GPIO 1), the unused standard-mode lanes (GPIO 2-3) and the pins with no SEP source (GPIO 4-7, 10, 54) are released, and on every transmit bit the transmit pin (GPIO 0) is driven; at least one cycle before the host enables its outputs has the SCK and CS# pins released. Fails on an inverted or tied output enable. The host may keep lane 0 driven into a receive segment, so lane 0's enable on receive bits is graded by `CHK-SEP-SPI-PAD-MAP` against the host, not here
|`CHK-SEP-SPI-PAD-MOSI` |the transmit pin sampled at the 40 transmit SCK rises gives the bytes 0x9F, 0x03, 0x00, 0x10, 0x00: the bytes the firmware pushes into TXDATA in FIFO order, the low byte of a word first (STATUS.BYTEORDER=1, OT :76-82), MSB first in a byte (OT :521-522). Fails when the transmit data does not reach GPIO 0, a lane is swapped, or the bit or byte order changes
|`CHK-SEP-SPI-PAD-RSP` |at each of the 32 receive SCK rises the bench device is driving and the host `spi_sd_i[1]` equals the driven bit, and the RX FIFO is popped exactly once in the window with a word equal to the driven word, the first received byte in RXDATA[7:0] (OT :76-82). Fails when the receive pin does not reach the host input, the response lanes are swapped or tied, or the host assembles a different word
|`CHK-SEP-SPI-PAD-IDLE` |no `core2pad_o` or `core2pad_en_o` bit of an SPI pin changes on a cycle where the host outputs do not change; the pre-transfer window (at least 200 cycles) and the post-transfer window (at least 2000 cycles) have no such change; SCK, CS# and the transmit pin each move as data and as output enable inside the transfer. Fails when a pin moves without the host, for example when another source drives it
|`CHK-SEP-SPI-PAD-XZ` |four-state simulator only: on every cycle of the window the bits of the 12 SPI pins in `core2pad_o`, `core2pad_en_o`, `pad2core_en_o`, `lsio_interface_select_o` and `pad2core_i`, the seven host nets and the RX FIFO handshake (and `rx_data` on a pop) read 0 or 1. Fails when an X or Z reaches a probed net, for example from an undriven or unreset pin path. Not run on Verilator, which is two-state
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_REAL_FW_SPI_OK`, `SEP_SPI_CONTROLLER_TXRX_OK`, `SEP_SPI_PAD_MAP_OK`, `SEP_SPI_PAD_SCK_COUNT_OK`, `SEP_SPI_PAD_FRAMING_OK`, `SEP_SPI_PAD_OE_OK`, `SEP_SPI_PAD_MOSI_OK`, `SEP_SPI_PAD_RSP_OK`, `SEP_SPI_PAD_IDLE_OK`, and on a four-state simulator `SEP_SPI_PAD_XZ_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-091-smu_sep_remap_test]]
===== 91 `smu_sep_remap_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_remap_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_remap` as a *stimulus generator*; the verdict is the testbench's read of the remap datapath

*Objective*: The AP and STEE output-remap region-0 offsets end up at the goldens the firmware names.

*Test Coverage*:

* Unlike the other real-firmware anchors this image is not self-checking: the remap writes are write-only, so `program_output_remap()` cannot fail and the compiler proves its fail loop dead (the linker then drops the symbol).
* Reaching the pass loop therefore proves only that the firmware ran to completion. The verdict has to come from the testbench.
* The testbench reads the offsets the remap datapath ended up with and compares them against the goldens named in the firmware. Both halves are required: park in the pass loop AND land the goldens.
* Non-vacuity is a real delta: the pre-run offsets are sampled and must differ from the goldens, so a datapath that was already at the golden value cannot pass.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Sample the AP and STEE region-0 offsets before the firmware runs and record them.
. Run the image and observe boot-ROM execution, ICCM execution and the pass loop.
. Re-read both offsets and compare each against the golden named in the firmware.
. Require both to have changed from their pre-run values, then emit the tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-REMAP-PROGRAM` |AP and STEE region-0 offsets both match their goldens and both changed from their pre-run values
|`CHK-SEP-REMAP-FRONTDOOR` |boot-ROM and ICCM execution seen and the pass loop reached
|`CHK-NONVAC` |the pre-run offsets differed from the goldens, so the compare could have failed
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_REAL_FW_REMAP_OK`, `SEP_OUTPUT_REMAP_GOLDEN_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-092-smu_sep_smc_notify_test]]
===== 92 `smu_sep_smc_notify_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_smc_notify_test`

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smc_notify`; SEP outbound AW counters and the SMU-boundary write counters as observables

*Objective*: The SEP outbound egress path is proven segment by segment, not inferred from the arriving magic.

*Test Coverage*:

* The image is minimal for exactly this: it opens the outbound filter over the mailbox, writes the two-word PASS magic, and does nothing else.
* `smu_sep_sanity_test` also ends in that magic, but only after HMAC and KMAC, so a failure there has several possible homes. Here the egress path is the only thing under test.
* The magic alone would not prove the path: it is decoded at the SMU boundary, and seeing it says the bytes arrived without saying how far they travelled as bus traffic.
* So the chain is walked instead -- SEP issues an outbound AW, the crossbar delivers a write at the SMU boundary, and only then the mailbox decodes PASS. Each segment is a separate assertion, so a break names the segment.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Boot the image and observe boot-ROM then ICCM execution.
. Count SEP outbound AW beats and require the count to advance.
. Count writes observed at the SMU boundary and require them to advance.
. Read the mailbox and require `TEST_MAGIC_PASS`, then emit the tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-EGRESS-CHAIN` |SEP outbound AW count advanced, SMU-boundary writes advanced, and the mailbox then decoded `TEST_MAGIC_PASS` -- each segment observed, not inferred from the magic alone
|`CHK-SEP-EGRESS-FRONTDOOR` |boot-ROM and ICCM execution both seen
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_REAL_FW_NOTIFY_OK`, `SEP_OUTBOUND_EGRESS_CHAIN_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-093-smu_sep_bidirect_test]]
===== 93 `smu_sep_bidirect_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_bidirect_test`

*BFM / Model*: two real firmwares against each other -- SEP `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_bidirect` and SMC `hw/sys/smu/dv/fw/tests/smu_sep_bidirect_arm`; SEP verdict classified by the terminal-loop PC the SEP settles on; loop addresses come from the staged `.sym`, so the contract survives a relink

*Objective*: SEP and SMC complete a bidirectional handshake across the SMU crossbar, with both directions load-bearing.

*Test Coverage*:

* The SEP half programs the SMU xbar SEP aperture, opens its outbound and inbound filter windows, read/write-checks SMC scratch8..11 across the crossbar, then trades four patterns with the SMC.
* The SMC half is the counterpart and also records how far it got in SMC scratch0.
* Both directions have to work for the SEP to reach its pass loop: SEP->SMC is proven by the scratch read/write checks and by the SMC observing `SEP_READY` and `SEP_ACK`; SMC->SEP by the SEP observing the two reply patterns in its own cold scratch. A one-way failure parks the SEP in its fail loop instead.
* The SMC phase marker is read for *diagnosis only* -- it answers "which side stopped", which a PC profile of the SEP alone cannot.
* On failure the sequence also reports the SEP aperture geometry, because SMC->SEP writes at `0x1080_2000` only fall inside it once the firmware has widened `SEP_REGION_SIZE`; at the reset value the crossbar silently delivers the reply to `ext_out` instead.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Run both images; classify the SEP by its terminal-loop PC as in the rest of the real-firmware suite.
. On an assertion failure, attribute the stall by reading the SMC phase marker and the SEP aperture/inbound-AW state before re-raising.
. On success, re-read the phase marker and aperture state and record them alongside the handshake token.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`+CHK-SEP_BIDIRECT-FRONTDOOR+` |first retired PC is the boot-ROM reset vector, boot-ROM and ICCM execution both seen
|`+CHK-SEP_BIDIRECT-VERDICT+` |the SEP reached its pass loop, which requires both handshake directions to have completed
|`CHK-SEP-SMC-HANDSHAKE` |SEP parks in `smu_bidirect_pass_loop` and, within 20000 clk_smu_i cycles of that, `smc_scratch_0_o` reads the SMC firmware's `SMC_PHASE_DONE` (0x5C000004) as a second, independent compare; an X sample or expiry fails. `SEP_SMC_HANDSHAKE_OK` is logged only after this compare; the SEP aperture/inbound-AW state is reported with it
|`CHK-NONVAC` |`boot_rom < iccm < pass_loop` ordering holds
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_REAL_FW_BIDIRECT_OK`, `SEP_SMC_HANDSHAKE_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-094-smu_sep_lcc_flow_test]]
===== 94 `smu_sep_lcc_flow_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_lcc_flow_test`

*BFM / Model*: the eFuse bank model (`hw/ip/efuse/dv/models/efuse_bank_model.sv`) holding the `+sep_efuse_hex` image the SEP sense reads; the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_lcc_flow` driving the DEMOTE registers; consumer-side observables at the SMC (`lc_state`), the DTP (`dbg_disable`, `dbg_disable.smc_jtag2axi`) and `feat_ctrl`; `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` and `sep_fuse_sense_skipped_o` at the wrapper boundary

*Objective*: The SEP lifecycle posture is earned by the SEP eFuse sense and then driven by firmware, and both movements are traced to every consumer as a delta off the pre-sense posture.

*Test Coverage*:

* The SEP LCC decides the chiplet posture and three consumers act on it: `lc_state` -> SMC, `dbg_disable` -> DTP, and `feat_ctrl`.
* A single static sample of `lc_state_o` proves the wire exists; it does not prove the LCC responds to anything and says nothing about the other two legs. This anchor adds the hardware half for the sense and for each firmware stage.
* The run carries no `+skip_fuse_sense` and no shadow preload: the SEP shadow registers are earned by the sense FSM from the `+sep_efuse_hex` image staged into the run directory (`build/firmware/preloads/sep_efuse_default.hex`, whose LC word is all-zero, so LC_STATE raw `0x0` and therefore TEST_DEV; the tracked file of the same basename under `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/efuse_preloads/` is a different image and is not the one loaded here), and the run fails if `sep_fuse_sense_skipped_o` reads 1 or `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` does not rise within its bound.
* The contract the consumers are compared against comes from `seq_lib/smu_lifecycle_table.py` for the state the image programs: `lc_state` `{~raw, raw}` at the SMC, every debug path open (`dbg_disable` `0x0000`, `dbg_disable.smc_jtag2axi` clear) at the DTP in TEST_DEV, with and without the demotes.
* Before the sense delivers the LC word `lc_state` reads `0x0f` (the INVALID encoding) and `dbg_disable` is not all-open. A monitor started before bring-up samples that posture at cold-reset release, logs every transition of `lc_state`, `dbg_disable` and `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` with its time, and samples the posture again after sense-done. Each sense leg is therefore a delta off the pre-sense posture and a compare against the table's value.
* The lock stage is what makes the demote stages non-vacuous: a register that simply stored whatever software wrote would satisfy every demote check. Only a *refused* write after lock shows the block implements the policy.
* *Not claimed*: a demote is not required to move `feat_ctrl`. In TEST_DEV the baseline is already `~(sip_dis | sys_dis)`, so the bits a demote forces high are high already and the write is a no-op. `feat_ctrl` is reported, not asserted on; the demote's effect is checked at `lcc_demote_state_*_o`, where it is unambiguous. The order of the LC word within the sense is not claimed either: `lc_state` may leave `0x0f` before `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` rises, and the post-sense compare is made after sense-done.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Start the sense monitor before bring-up; at cold-reset release require `sep_fuse_sense_skipped_o` = 0, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o` = 0, `lc_state` = `0x0f` and `dbg_disable` not all-open, and record that posture as the pre-sense baseline.
. Decode the lifecycle state from the `+sep_efuse_hex` image and take the consumer contract for it from the lifecycle table.
. Wait, bounded, for `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`; sample the consumers after it and compare them against the contract.
. Run the firmware through its four stages -- FEAT_CTRL readable, DEMOTE_1 accepted and read back, DEMOTE_2 accepted and read back, and a later clear refused by the DEMOTE_1 lock.
. Re-read each consumer: both demote outputs moved off the baseline, and `lc_state` / `dbg_disable` hold the table's value for the state with both demotes set.
. Require the firmware to have reached its pass loop, then emit the tokens.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-SENSE` |the SEP eFuse sense ran (`sep_fuse_sense_skipped_o` = 0) and completed within its bound; SMC `lc_state` moved off `0x0f` to the table's value for the sensed image and DTP `dbg_disable` moved from not-all-open to the table's value
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-FW-STAGES` |the firmware cleared all four stages on-chip: FEAT_CTRL readable, DEMOTE_1 and DEMOTE_2 each accepted and read back, and the DEMOTE_1 lock refused a later clear
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-FANOUT` |demote1 and demote2 both moved at `lcc_demote_state_*_o`; SMC `lc_state` and DTP `dbg_disable` hold the table's value for the state with both demotes set; `feat_ctrl` is reported, not asserted on
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-NONVAC` |every sense claim is a delta against the pre-sense posture sampled at cold-reset release *and* a compare against the lifecycle table's value for the sensed image, and the write-once lock rules out plain storage
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Evidence tokens: `SEP_LCC_SENSE_OBSERVED_OK`, `SEP_LCC_FW_FLOW_OK`, `SEP_LCC_FANOUT_TO_SMC_DTP_OK`. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_real_fw`.

'''''
[[sec-095-smu_sep_lcc_state_test_dev_test]]
===== 95 `smu_sep_lcc_state_test_dev_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_lcc_state_test_dev_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; one image of the shared body `smu_sep_lcc_state_matrix_test.py`)

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_lcc_flow` driving the DEMOTE registers; eFuse shadow preload `hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/sep_efuse_shadow_lc_test_dev.preload` supplied by the testbench

*Objective*: Lifecycle decode for this state: `lc_state` must reach `0xf0` at the SMC, with `dbg_disable` clear at the DTP.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smu_sep_lcc_flow_test` proves the firmware-driven demote reaches every consumer, but it runs in one lifecycle state, so it cannot tell a real decode from a wire. This family runs the same firmware against different eFuse images.
* The eFuse image is the one LCC input firmware cannot drive -- it is sampled before the CPU runs -- so it comes from the testbench side; everything downstream of it is still driven by the firmware.
* Expectations arrive as plusargs rather than being derived in the sequence, so each testlist entry states the contract for its own image: `+lcc_expect_lc_state`, `+lcc_expect_demote_effective`, `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled`.
* Per Table 50 the demotes gate `feat_ctrl[15:0]` and `feat_ctrl[31:16]` in TEST_DEV and PROD but have no entry in PROD_END. The check that can fail is a delta against the reset baseline.
* TEST_DEV's baseline `feat_ctrl` is already `~(sip_dis | sys_dis)`, so demote-open is *not* a delta here; the sequence says so explicitly and PROD / PROD_END are the discriminating cases.
* This entry's contract: `+lcc_expect_lc_state=0xf0`, `+lcc_expect_demote_effective=1`, `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled=0`.
* Runs in the `sep_rtl_no_shadow` mode so the entry's own preload -- not the default shadow image -- is the one sampled.
* The boundary outputs the compares read -- `smc_lc_state_in_o`, `lcc_dbg_disable_o`, `lcc_feat_ctrl_o`, `lcc_demote_state_{1,2}_o` -- are sampled X-strict: an X or Z sample fails the leaf instead of reading as the passing value.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Preload the SEP eFuse shadow registers from this entry's image, before the CPU runs.
. Sample the reset baseline of `feat_ctrl[31:0]`, `lc_state` and `dbg_disable`.
. Run the same `sep_smu_lcc_flow` firmware and require it to reach its pass loop.
. Compare `lc_state` against `0xf0`, the post-run `feat_ctrl[31:0]` state against `+lcc_expect_demote_effective=1`, and `dbg_disable` against `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled=0`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-STATE-DECODE` |this eFuse image produced SMC `lc_state` `0xf0` as contracted -- the LCC is decoding the image, not passing a fixed value
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-STATE-PROFILE` |`feat_ctrl[31:0]` and `dbg_disable` `0` match Table 50 for this state, with the demote asserted at the boundary either way
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_lifecycle`.

'''''
[[sec-096-smu_sep_lcc_state_prod_test]]
===== 96 `smu_sep_lcc_state_prod_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_lcc_state_prod_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; one image of the shared body `smu_sep_lcc_state_matrix_test.py`)

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_lcc_flow` driving the DEMOTE registers; eFuse shadow preload `hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/sep_efuse_shadow_lc_prod.preload` supplied by the testbench

*Objective*: Lifecycle decode for this state: `lc_state` must reach `0xe1` at the SMC, with `dbg_disable` clear at the DTP.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smu_sep_lcc_flow_test` proves the firmware-driven demote reaches every consumer, but it runs in one lifecycle state, so it cannot tell a real decode from a wire. This family runs the same firmware against different eFuse images.
* The eFuse image is the one LCC input firmware cannot drive -- it is sampled before the CPU runs -- so it comes from the testbench side; everything downstream of it is still driven by the firmware.
* Expectations arrive as plusargs rather than being derived in the sequence, so each testlist entry states the contract for its own image: `+lcc_expect_lc_state`, `+lcc_expect_demote_effective`, `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled`.
* Per Table 50 the demotes gate `feat_ctrl[15:0]` and `feat_ctrl[31:16]` in TEST_DEV and PROD but have no entry in PROD_END. The check that can fail is a delta against the reset baseline.
* PROD is one of the two discriminating cases: the baseline starts closed and the demote must open `feat_ctrl[15:0]`, so this is a real closed-to-open delta.
* This entry's contract: `+lcc_expect_lc_state=0xe1`, `+lcc_expect_demote_effective=1`, `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled=0`.
* Runs in the `sep_rtl_no_shadow` mode so the entry's own preload -- not the default shadow image -- is the one sampled.
* The boundary outputs the compares read -- `smc_lc_state_in_o`, `lcc_dbg_disable_o`, `lcc_feat_ctrl_o`, `lcc_demote_state_{1,2}_o` -- are sampled X-strict: an X or Z sample fails the leaf instead of reading as the passing value.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Preload the SEP eFuse shadow registers from this entry's image, before the CPU runs.
. Sample the reset baseline of `feat_ctrl[31:0]`, `lc_state` and `dbg_disable`.
. Run the same `sep_smu_lcc_flow` firmware and require it to reach its pass loop.
. Compare `lc_state` against `0xe1`, the post-run `feat_ctrl[31:0]` state against `+lcc_expect_demote_effective=1`, and `dbg_disable` against `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled=0`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-STATE-DECODE` |this eFuse image produced SMC `lc_state` `0xe1` as contracted -- the LCC is decoding the image, not passing a fixed value
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-STATE-PROFILE` |`feat_ctrl[31:0]` and `dbg_disable` `0` match Table 50 for this state, with the demote asserted at the boundary either way
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_lifecycle`.

'''''

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_lcc_flow` driving the DEMOTE registers; eFuse shadow preload `hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/sep_efuse_shadow_lc_prod_end.preload` supplied by the testbench

*Objective*: Lifecycle decode for this state: `lc_state` must reach `0x78` at the SMC, with `dbg_disable` asserted at the DTP.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smu_sep_lcc_flow_test` proves the firmware-driven demote reaches every consumer, but it runs in one lifecycle state, so it cannot tell a real decode from a wire. This family runs the same firmware against different eFuse images.
* The eFuse image is the one LCC input firmware cannot drive -- it is sampled before the CPU runs -- so it comes from the testbench side; everything downstream of it is still driven by the firmware.

* PROD_END is the other discriminating case: Table 50 has no demote entry, so the same firmware must leave `feat_ctrl` closed, and `dbg_disable` is expected to assert.

. Run the same `sep_smu_lcc_flow` firmware and require it to reach its pass loop.
. Compare `lc_state` against `0x78`, the post-run `feat_ctrl[31:0]` state against `+lcc_expect_demote_effective=0`, and `dbg_disable` against `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled=1`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-STATE-DECODE` |this eFuse image produced SMC `lc_state` `0x78` as contracted -- the LCC is decoding the image, not passing a fixed value
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-STATE-PROFILE` |`feat_ctrl[31:0]` and `dbg_disable` `1` match Table 50 for this state, with the demote asserted at the boundary either way
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_lifecycle`.

[[sec-098-smu_sep_lcc_state_rma_chiplet_test]]
===== 98 `smu_sep_lcc_state_rma_chiplet_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_lcc_state_rma_chiplet_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; one image of the shared body `smu_sep_lcc_state_matrix_test.py`)

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_lcc_flow` driving the DEMOTE registers; eFuse shadow preload `hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/sep_efuse_shadow_lc_rma_chiplet.preload` supplied by the testbench

*Objective*: Lifecycle decode for this state: `lc_state` must reach `0x96` at the SMC, with `dbg_disable` clear at the DTP.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smu_sep_lcc_flow_test` proves the firmware-driven demote reaches every consumer, but it runs in one lifecycle state, so it cannot tell a real decode from a wire. This family runs the same firmware against different eFuse images.
* The eFuse image is the one LCC input firmware cannot drive -- it is sampled before the CPU runs -- so it comes from the testbench side; everything downstream of it is still driven by the firmware.
* Expectations arrive as plusargs rather than being derived in the sequence, so each testlist entry states the contract for its own image: `+lcc_expect_lc_state`, `+lcc_expect_demote_effective`, `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled`.
* Per Table 50 the demotes gate `feat_ctrl[15:0]` and `feat_ctrl[31:16]` in TEST_DEV and PROD but have no entry in PROD_END. The check that can fail is a delta against the reset baseline.
* RMA_CHIPLET's `feat_ctrl` is all-ones by state, so like TEST_DEV it cannot show a closed-to-open demote; a post-run open low-32 does not prove the write did anything here. The `lc_state` decode is the load-bearing half.
* This entry's contract: `+lcc_expect_lc_state=0x96`, `+lcc_expect_demote_effective=1`, `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled=0`.
* Runs in the `sep_rtl_no_shadow` mode so the entry's own preload -- not the default shadow image -- is the one sampled.
* The boundary outputs the compares read -- `smc_lc_state_in_o`, `lcc_dbg_disable_o`, `lcc_feat_ctrl_o`, `lcc_demote_state_{1,2}_o` -- are sampled X-strict: an X or Z sample fails the leaf instead of reading as the passing value.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Preload the SEP eFuse shadow registers from this entry's image, before the CPU runs.
. Sample the reset baseline of `feat_ctrl[31:0]`, `lc_state` and `dbg_disable`.
. Run the same `sep_smu_lcc_flow` firmware and require it to reach its pass loop.
. Compare `lc_state` against `0x96`, the post-run `feat_ctrl[31:0]` state against `+lcc_expect_demote_effective=1`, and `dbg_disable` against `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled=0`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-STATE-DECODE` |this eFuse image produced SMC `lc_state` `0x96` as contracted -- the LCC is decoding the image, not passing a fixed value
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-STATE-PROFILE` |`feat_ctrl[31:0]` and `dbg_disable` `0` match Table 50 for this state, with the demote asserted at the boundary either way
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_lifecycle`.

'''''
[[sec-099-smu_sep_dbg_gating_test_dev_test]]
===== 99 `smu_sep_dbg_gating_test_dev_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_dbg_gating_test_dev_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; one image of the shared body `smu_sep_dbg_gating_test.py`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` on the primary TAP; the SEP runs the `hello_world` DV image; eFuse shadow preload `hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/sep_efuse_shadow_lc_test_dev.preload`

*Objective*: Debug is open in this state: the JTAG2AXI bridge must launch the transaction on the debug AXI port.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smu_sep_lcc_state_prod_end_test` proves `dbg_disable` asserts in PROD_END. That is the signal, not the effect. This family closes the gap by issuing a real JTAG2AXI transaction and counting whether the bridge launched it on the DTP -> SMC debug AXI port.
* Per `jtag2axi.sv` the bridge's update is gated by `!security_disable_i`, and `jtag_intf_unit.sv` wires that from `dbg_disable.smc_jtag2axi`. So with debug disabled the transaction must never reach AXI at all -- not merely be answered with an error.
* Both polarities are needed and each entry states its own via `+dbg_expect_gated`.
* TEST_DEV leaves debug open with a blank eFuse because `feat_ctrl` is `~(sip_dis | sys_dis)` there, so `sip_debug` is already set. This is the permissive polarity.
* The permissive entry is what makes the gated one meaningful: on its own, "no AXI transaction" is equally consistent with a TAP that never worked, a wrong scan payload, or a probe watching the wrong port.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Preload the SEP eFuse shadow registers from this entry's image.
. Bring the primary TAP up and sample the DTP -> SMC debug AXI AW and AR counts.
. Issue a JTAG2AXI single-op over the TAP.
. Re-sample the counts and compare the movement against `+dbg_expect_gated=0`, alongside `lc_state` and `dbg_disable`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-DBG-OPEN` |`lc_state` equals the TEST_DEV word of `seq_lib/smu_lifecycle_table.py` (spec raw encoding, RDL `{~raw, raw}` packing), `dbg_disable` is all-clear and its `smc_jtag2axi` field is 0, and the bridge launched the transaction: AW and AR both advanced
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_lifecycle`.

'''''
[[sec-100-smu_sep_dbg_gating_prod_end_test]]
===== 100 `smu_sep_dbg_gating_prod_end_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_dbg_gating_prod_end_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; one image of the shared body `smu_sep_dbg_gating_test.py`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` on the primary TAP; the SEP runs the `hello_world` DV image; eFuse shadow preload `hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/sep_efuse_shadow_lc_prod_end.preload`

*Objective*: Debug is gated in this state: the JTAG2AXI bridge must not launch the transaction at all.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smu_sep_lcc_state_prod_end_test` proves `dbg_disable` asserts in PROD_END. That is the signal, not the effect. This family closes the gap by issuing a real JTAG2AXI transaction and counting whether the bridge launched it on the DTP -> SMC debug AXI port.
* Per `jtag2axi.sv` the bridge's update is gated by `!security_disable_i`, and `jtag_intf_unit.sv` wires that from `dbg_disable.smc_jtag2axi`. So with debug disabled the transaction must never reach AXI at all -- not merely be answered with an error.
* Both polarities are needed and each entry states its own via `+dbg_expect_gated`.
* PROD does *not* leave debug open -- its `feat_ctrl` is zero except `func_reserved` until a demote opens the window -- so PROD with a payload that never demotes has debug disabled, same as PROD_END. This entry is the gated polarity.
* The permissive entry is what makes the gated one meaningful: on its own, "no AXI transaction" is equally consistent with a TAP that never worked, a wrong scan payload, or a probe watching the wrong port.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Preload the SEP eFuse shadow registers from this entry's image.
. Bring the primary TAP up and sample the DTP -> SMC debug AXI AW and AR counts.
. Issue a JTAG2AXI single-op over the TAP.
. Re-sample the counts and compare the movement against `+dbg_expect_gated=1`, alongside `lc_state` and `dbg_disable`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-DBG-GATED` |`lc_state` equals the PROD_END word of `seq_lib/smu_lifecycle_table.py`, the `dbg_disable.smc_jtag2axi` field is 1 (path-gating Case 2 with DBG_1/DBG_2 both disabled), and the bridge launched nothing: AW and AR both unchanged
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_lifecycle`.

'''''
[[sec-101-smu_dtp_sep_smc_chain_prod_test]]
===== 101 `smu_dtp_sep_smc_chain_prod_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_dtp_sep_smc_chain_prod_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; one image of the shared body `smu_dtp_sep_smc_chain_test.py`)

*BFM / Model*: three subsystems live: SMC firmware from ROM, SEP `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_lcc_flow`, and `ocah_jtag_vip` driving a JTAG2AXI single-op; eFuse shadow preload `hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/sep_efuse_shadow_lc_prod.preload`

*Objective*: SEP decides, DTP enforces, SMC serves: the demote opens debug and the JTAG2AXI read returns the SMC's live scratch value.

*Test Coverage*:

* The other anchors each cover part of this. `smu_sep_lcc_flow_test` has the SEP firmware drive the posture but only reads the DTP- and SMC-facing signals. `smu_sep_dbg_gating_*` proves the DTP acts on the posture, but its SEP payload is `hello_world` and its SMC end is only a transaction count. Neither has all three subsystems doing something.
* Here they all do: the SMC boots from ROM and writes its scratch0 marker; the SEP firmware sets DEMOTE_1/2; the DTP is asked for a JTAG2AXI single-op read of that SMC scratch address; and the SMC answers.
* Causality comes from running the identical firmware and the identical JTAG operation under two eFuse images, one per entry in this pair.
* PROD: Table 50 lets the demote open `feat_ctrl[15:0]`, so debug opens and the read must complete and return the SMC's live scratch value.
* The returned data is compared against the testbench's own view of SMC scratch0 rather than a hard-coded constant, so the check follows whatever the SMC firmware actually wrote.
* This entry's contract: `+chain_expect_served=1`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Preload the SEP eFuse shadow registers from this entry's image.
. Let the SMC boot from ROM and write its scratch0 marker; read that marker on the testbench side.
. Run the SEP firmware so it sets DEMOTE_1/2.
. Issue the JTAG2AXI single-op read of the SMC scratch address over the primary TAP.
. Require the read to complete and compare the returned word against the marker the testbench read.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-DTP-SEP-SMC-CHAIN` |`lc_state` equals the PROD word of `seq_lib/smu_lifecycle_table.py`, `dbg_disable` is all-clear after the demote, the DTP launched the read, and the SMC answered OKAY with the value its own firmware wrote
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_chain`.

'''''
[[sec-102-smu_dtp_sep_smc_chain_prod_end_test]]
===== 102 `smu_dtp_sep_smc_chain_prod_end_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_dtp_sep_smc_chain_prod_end_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; one image of the shared body `smu_dtp_sep_smc_chain_test.py`)

*BFM / Model*: three subsystems live: SMC firmware from ROM, SEP `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_lcc_flow`, and `ocah_jtag_vip` driving a JTAG2AXI single-op; eFuse shadow preload `hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/sep_efuse_shadow_lc_prod_end.preload`

*Objective*: The same firmware and the same JTAG operation under an eFuse image that ignores the demote: debug stays disabled and the read must not reach AXI.

*Test Coverage*:

* The other anchors each cover part of this. `smu_sep_lcc_flow_test` has the SEP firmware drive the posture but only reads the DTP- and SMC-facing signals. `smu_sep_dbg_gating_*` proves the DTP acts on the posture, but its SEP payload is `hello_world` and its SMC end is only a transaction count. Neither has all three subsystems doing something.
* Here they all do: the SMC boots from ROM and writes its scratch0 marker; the SEP firmware sets DEMOTE_1/2; the DTP is asked for a JTAG2AXI single-op read of that SMC scratch address; and the SMC answers.
* Causality comes from running the identical firmware and the identical JTAG operation under two eFuse images, one per entry in this pair.
* PROD_END: Table 50 has no demote entry, so the same demote changes nothing, debug stays disabled and the read must not reach AXI at all. With the demote ignored the chain breaks, which is what makes the SEP's participation load-bearing in the PROD half.
* The returned data is compared against the testbench's own view of SMC scratch0 rather than a hard-coded constant, so the check follows whatever the SMC firmware actually wrote.
* This entry's contract: `+chain_expect_served=0`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Preload the SEP eFuse shadow registers from this entry's image.
. Let the SMC boot from ROM and write its scratch0 marker; read that marker on the testbench side.
. Run the SEP firmware so it sets DEMOTE_1/2.
. Issue the JTAG2AXI single-op read of the SMC scratch address over the primary TAP.
. Require the bridge to have launched nothing -- the debug AXI AW/AR counts must not move.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-DTP-SEP-SMC-CHAIN-BLOCKED` |same firmware and same JTAG operation, but PROD_END ignores the demote: `lc_state` equals the PROD_END word of `seq_lib/smu_lifecycle_table.py`, `dbg_disable.smc_jtag2axi` stays 1, and the bridge launched nothing
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_chain`.

'''''
[[sec-103-smu_smc_cool_reset_pin_test]]
===== 103 `smu_smc_cool_reset_pin_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_cool_reset_pin_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (SMC fabric JTAG2AXI for the CSR reads); TB pin drive on `rst_cool_n_from_pin_i` and the four DFT done/pass straps; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: A 28-`clk_ref` pulse on `rst_cool_n_from_pin_i` is filtered and a hold past that pulse asserts the SMC primary reset; the four DFT done/pass straps are paired with their set-only DFX_CTRL.STATUS_SMU fields through that reset.

*Test Coverage*:

* Both halves of the pin contract `hw/sys/smu/doc/port_table.adoc` gives `rst_cool_n_from_pin_i` (cool reset into the SMC reset unit): a 28-`clk_ref` pulse leaves the SMC running, a hold that lasts past that pulse asserts the SMC primary reset, and the SMC leaves reset after the pin releases. The table does not state a deglitch width; 28 `clk_ref` is the pulse this leaf proves is filtered. The reset is observed on `obs_smc_rst_n_o`, the bench's view of the SMC primary reset.
* `mem_repair_done_i`, `mem_repair_success_i`, `mbist_done_i` and `mbist_pass_i` against the set-only DFX_CTRL.STATUS_SMU fields that mirror them. The bench asserts the straps at bring-up, so the fields are already set when any leaf starts; this leaf holds the straps low across the pin reset, reads the fields at their reset value, and then raises the pairs one at a time so each field is attributed to its own strap. The fields are required to hold when the straps drop again.
* The cool reset is asserted through the pin only; `ISOLATE_REQ_FLR_RESET_COUNTER_VALUE` stays at its reset value of zero, so the FLR-driven cool reset is not part of this leaf.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read DFX_CTRL.STATUS_SMU and require the four done/pass fields set and `obs_smc_rst_n_o` high.
. Hold `rst_cool_n_from_pin_i` low for 28 `clk_ref`, sampling `obs_smc_rst_n_o` every `clk_ref` during the pulse and for 128 cycles after release.
. Take the four straps low, hold the pin low, and wait for `obs_smc_rst_n_o` to fall; require the fall no earlier than the pulse length that was filtered, and require the reset to stay asserted while the pin is held.
. Release the pin and wait for `obs_smc_rst_n_o` to rise; re-check the SMC JTAG2AXI path is open.
. Read STATUS_SMU with the straps still low, raise the memory-repair pair and read, raise the MBIST pair and read, take all four low again and read.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-COOL-PIN-DEGLITCH` |a 28-clk_ref rst_cool_n_from_pin_i pulse leaves the SMC primary reset released, during the pulse and after it
|`CHK-SMU-COOL-PIN-RESET` |rst_cool_n_from_pin_i held past that pulse asserts the SMC primary reset no earlier than 28 clk_ref, holds it while low, and the SMC leaves reset after the pin releases
|`CHK-SMU-DFT-DONE-STATUS` |DFX_CTRL.STATUS_SMU reads mem_repair_done, mem_repair_success, mbist_done and mbist_pass clear after a primary reset with the straps low; each pair sets when its straps rise and holds when they drop again
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `smc_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''
[[sec-104-smu_smc_ss_reset_ctrl_sweep_test]]
===== 104 `smu_smc_ss_reset_ctrl_sweep_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_ss_reset_ctrl_sweep_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (SMC fabric JTAG2AXI for the CSR reads and writes); TB observe words assembled from `ss_reset_ctrl_o[i].<field>`; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: The seven SMC reset-unit registers that own the fields of `ss_reset_ctrl_o` drive the 32 subsystem elements lane for lane, every field bit is driven both ways, and a `SS_COLD_RESET_LOCK` bit blocks its own cold-reset lane while the rest of the write lands.

*Test Coverage*:

* `reset_unit.rdl` gives each `reset_ctrl_t` field one 32-bit register whose bit i belongs to subsystem i: `SS_COLD_RESET_N`, `SS_WARM_RESET_N`, `SS_CONFIG_HOLD`, `SS_SRAM_HOLD`, `SS_CRITICAL_HOLD`, `SS_DEBUG_HOLD` and `SS_FORCE_TO_REF_CLK`. Each register is swept and each sweep is observed on its own field of all 32 elements.
* Expected values are the masks and reset constants of the generated `hw/sys/smc/regs/gen/c/blocks/reset_unit.h`; the two reset fields are active low and reset opposite ways, cold reset asserted (`0x0`) and warm reset released (`0xFFFFFFFF`).
* The pattern set is the five binary-code patterns and their complements, so lane i answers with a signature unique to i and a permutation between register bit and subsystem element is visible. The pairs also put both values on every bit, and each sweep starts and ends at the register's reset value, so all 32x7 field bits are observed rising and falling.
* `SS_COLD_RESET_LOCK` is `onwrite = woset`: a set bit cannot be cleared and blocks writes to its own lane of `SS_COLD_RESET_N`. That leg runs after the sweep and leaves the locked lane at the reset value.
* `ss_reset_ctrl_o` leaves the wrapper and feeds nothing the bench depends on, so the sweep cannot disturb the JTAG2AXI path the CSR accesses use.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read the seven registers and `SS_COLD_RESET_LOCK` and require the reset values `reset_unit.h` states; sample each field's boundary word and require the same value on all 32 lanes.
. For each register in turn, write the ten lane-signature patterns and then the register's reset value, reading the register back and sampling its boundary word after each write.
. Set `SS_COLD_RESET_LOCK` bit 0, read it back, write zero to it and require it to stay set; write all ones to `SS_COLD_RESET_N` and require lane 0 to hold and every other lane to follow, at the register and at the boundary; restore the reset value.
. Reconcile the boundary samples against the register states the sweep left behind, and require every field bit to have been observed both rising and falling.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-SSRST-RESET` |each of the seven reset-unit registers owning an `ss_reset_ctrl_o` field, and SS_COLD_RESET_LOCK, reads the reset value `reset_unit.h` states, and the boundary word carries that value on all 32 lanes
|`CHK-SMU-SSRST-LANE` |`ss_reset_ctrl_o[i].<field>` carries bit i of the register that owns the field through five binary-code patterns and their complements, so each lane answers with a signature unique to its index, and back to the RDL reset value
|`CHK-SMU-SSRST-LOCK` |a set SS_COLD_RESET_LOCK bit cannot be cleared and blocks its own lane of SS_COLD_RESET_N while the same write reaches every unlocked lane, at the register and at the boundary
|`CHK-SMU-SSRST-TOGGLE` |`ss_reset_ctrl_o` was sampled once per register state the sweep left behind, and all 32x7 field bits were observed both rising and falling
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `smc_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-105-smu_smc_efuse_shadow_sense_reset_test]]
===== 105 `smu_smc_efuse_shadow_sense_reset_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_efuse_shadow_sense_reset_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: `efuse_bank_model` loaded from `dv/assets/smc_efuse_all_ones.hex`; TB pin drive of `rst_cold_ni`; `elaboration` firmware mode; run mode `sep_rtl_fuse_sense`, which leaves `+skip_fuse_sense` unset

*Objective*: After a real SMC fuse sense, `smc_shadow_regs_o` carries the sensed eFuse image at every register offset `smc_addr.h` gives the map, and it reads the zero every `smc_efuse_map.rdl` field resets to while `rst_cold_ni` is held; the image programs every fuse bit, so both samples are taken on the full width of the port.

*Test Coverage*:

* The sense is the claim, so the leaf runs on the fuse-sense run mode and requires `+skip_fuse_sense` to be absent; `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` has to be observed rising 0 -> 1, and a run that begins with it already high fails.
* The expected value comes from the image file the leaf loads and from `smc_addr.h`, both DV-owned; no RTL package or module supplies a golden or a walk.
* `SMC_TOP_SMC_EFUSE_MAP_SIZE` sets the map at 1024 bytes and the fuse word at 4 bytes, so fuse word `i` is shadow bits `32i+31..32i`. The register base addresses in `smc_addr.h` are required to tile that map with no hole and no overlap, and each register's slice of the port is compared against its own bytes of the image: LOCKS, JTAG_PUBLIC_IDENTITY, the nine I2C_I3C_ID elements, SMC_CONFIG, OCCP_TRANSPORT_TIMEOUT and the twenty-eight SPARE regions.
* Every field of every register in `smc_efuse_map.rdl` resets to `0x0`, so the reset value of the whole map is zero; that is the value required before the sense completes and again while the cold reset is held, each over a 64-cycle window rather than at one instant.
* The image sets all 8192 fuse bits, which the leaf requires of it before it compares anything. Both directions are then counted: the sense has to move all 8192 bits 0 -> 1 and the cold reset all 8192 bits 1 -> 0, so a port stuck at either value fails rather than passing on a partial match.
* A uniform image cannot distinguish one word of the map from another. The word-for-word claim here is that each register's slice carries its own bytes of the image at the port's full width; distinct data at named offsets is placed by `smu_dtp_otp_smc_map_rw_test` and `smu_otp_vs_fabric_map_race_test`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring the wrapper up on the fuse-sense run mode and require `+skip_fuse_sense` to be absent and `smc_shadow_regs` to be as wide as the map size declares.
. Read the image `+smc_efuse_hex` names, require it to program every bit of the map, and sample `smc_shadow_regs` before the sense completes.
. Wait for `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` to rise, then compare each register's slice of `smc_shadow_regs` against its bytes of the image, count the bits that moved 0 -> 1, and require the port to hold that value for 64 clk_smu.
. Assert `rst_cold_ni`, wait for `smc_fuse_sense_done_o` to fall, and require `smc_shadow_regs` to read the map reset value, all 8192 bits having moved 1 -> 0.
. Require that reset value to hold for a further 64 clk_smu with `rst_cold_ni` still asserted.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-EFUSE-SHADOW-SENSE` |with `+skip_fuse_sense` unset, smc_shadow_regs carries the whole SMC_TOP_SMC_EFUSE_MAP_SIZE-wide sensed image, register slice by register slice at the byte offsets smc_addr.h gives them, and holds it after the sense
|`CHK-SMU-EFUSE-SHADOW-RESET` |smc_shadow_regs reads the zero every smc_efuse_map.rdl field resets to, both before the sense completes and for the whole time rst_cold_ni is held after it
|`CHK-SMU-EFUSE-SHADOW-TOGGLE` |the image programs every bit of the map, and every one of them is observed going 0 -> 1 on the sense and 1 -> 0 on the cold reset
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `smc_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-106-smu_smc_boundary_lane_sweep_test]]
===== 106 `smu_smc_boundary_lane_sweep_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_smc_boundary_lane_sweep_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip` (SMC fabric JTAG2AXI for the CSR reads and writes); TB pin drive on the external interrupt vector and the GPIO pad bus; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: Every lane of the multi-lane SMC-facing wrapper buses is driven both ways and compared against the SMC register or interrupt vector that owns it, and each aggregate names the lane count it expects.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smu_smc_boundary_io_test` proves each of these contracts on one or two lanes. This anchor takes the same contracts across every lane, so no lane of any of these buses rests on a neighbour's result.
* The 256 external interrupt lanes on the external-interrupt slice of `cpu_interrupts_o`; the 32 isolation-request and 32 subsystem-configuration lanes of the reset unit; the 32 outbound mailbox interrupt lanes; the 65 GPIO pads on `gpio_interrupt_o` and on their `DATA_CTRL.PAD2CORE` mirrors; and the four UART interrupt lanes.
* Every lane count comes from the generated SMC register header -- the enumerated outbound mailbox instances, `SMC_TOP_GPIO_INTF_DATA_CTRL_NUM`, the UART instance count and the reset-unit field widths -- or from the DV-owned port table in `seq_lib/smu_compose_helpers.py`, and the bench-side width is required to match it before any lane is driven, so a narrowed port fails rather than reducing the sweep.
* Each group ends in an aggregate that states its lane count, so a bus that never moved fails on the count rather than passing on an empty sweep.
* `smc_global_base_o` and `smc_region_size_o` are out of scope: the SMC aperture routes the bench's own JTAG2AXI accesses, and `smc_base_config.rdl` constrains `REGION_SIZE.size` to a non-zero power of two that `LOCAL_BASE` is aligned to, so no all-lanes sweep of either port is a legal stimulus.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Drive `smc_ext_interrupts_i` all-ones, all-zeros and both alternating patterns, sampling the external-interrupt slice of `cpu_interrupts_o` after each.
. Write RESET_UNIT.ISOLATE_REQ_REG all-ones, both alternating patterns and zero, reading the register back and `isolate_req_o` after each.
. Read SS_CONFIG_LOCK, then write RESET_UNIT.SS_CONFIG all-ones, both alternating patterns and its RDL reset value, reading the register back and `ss_config_o` after each.
. For each outbound mailbox in turn, enable IRQEN.WTIRQ and push one outbound word, and require exactly that bit of `smc_ext_mailbox_interrupts_o` to rise; then clear IRQEN mailbox by mailbox and require exactly that bit to fall.
. Arm every GPIO interface away from its LSIO owner as an active-high level, drive every pad high, and read `gpio_interrupt_o` and `DATA_CTRL.PAD2CORE` on every interface; drive every pad low and read both again.
. For each UART instance in turn, set IER.ETBEI, require its own `uart_interrupt_o` lane alone to rise, read IIR for the pending flag and the transmitter-holding-register-empty code, and require the lane to fall on that read.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SMU-LANE-EXT-IRQ` |all 256 smc_ext_interrupts_i lanes appear bit for bit on the external-interrupt slice of cpu_interrupts_o for all-ones, all-zeros and both alternating patterns, and every lane is observed both high and low
|`CHK-SMU-LANE-ISOLATE-REQ` |isolate_req_o carries the RESET_UNIT.ISOLATE_REQ_REG software term on all 32 lanes, each lane observed set and cleared against the CSR read-back
|`CHK-SMU-LANE-SS-CONFIG` |with SS_CONFIG_LOCK read open, ss_config_o carries RESET_UNIT.SS_CONFIG on all 32 lanes for all-ones, both alternating patterns and the RDL reset value it is restored to
|`CHK-SMU-LANE-MBX-IRQ` |each of the 32 outbound mailboxes raises its own smc_ext_mailbox_interrupts_o bit through IRQEN.WTIRQ and an outbound push, and clearing IRQEN retires that bit alone
|`CHK-SMU-LANE-GPIO` |with all 65 pads taken from their LSIO owners and armed as active-high levels, driving every pad raises every gpio_interrupt_o lane and sets DATA_CTRL.PAD2CORE on every interface, and both follow the pads back down
|`CHK-SMU-LANE-UART-IRQ` |each of the four UART instances raises uart_interrupt_o on its own lane from IER.ETBEI, reports the transmitter-holding-register-empty code in IIR, and retires that lane on the IIR read
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `smc_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''

[[sec-107-smu_axi_in_attribute_sweep_test]]
===== 107 `smu_axi_in_attribute_sweep_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_axi_in_attribute_sweep_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_axi_vip` master on the flat `ext_in_*` pins and the shared responder on `u_axi_out_if`; `ocah_jtag_vip` for the inbound filter entries, the SMC aperture and the outbound write; `elaboration` firmware mode

*Objective*: Every AW/AR attribute the inbound SMN port carries is answered as the traffic-filter specification assigns, and one SMC write leaves the chiplet on `smu_axi_out`.

*Test Coverage*:

* `hw/ip/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc` "Match, Then Permit" lists the whole match condition: `entry_enabled`, the address range after granule widening, `prot[1] == allow_ns`, the source ID, and `allow_burst = 1 or AxLEN = 0`. AxREGION, AxQOS, AxCACHE, AxLOCK, AxSIZE and AxBURST appear in none of them, so an entry that admits a transaction admits it at every value of those fields, and the sweep compares the response and the returned data against that.
* With one entry per `prot[1]` polarity over the probe page, all eight AxPROT encodings are admitted on AR and on AW; `prot[0]` and `prot[2]` take no part in the match, so no encoding may differ. AxREGION, AxQOS and AxCACHE at `0x0` and `0xF` and AxLOCK at `0` and `1` are driven on both channels, and each read returns the VERSION_LO RDL reset value while each write is read back.
* AxSIZE `0`..`3` is every legal encoding on a 64-bit port, and each read returns the byte slice of the VERSION_LO RDL reset value the size selects. AxSIZE[2] is not driven: an AxSIZE above 3 exceeds the data bus width and is not a legal AXI4 transfer here.
* `allow_burst = 0` narrows the granule to the 8-byte data bus and drops every `AxLEN != 0` out of the match, so the same entries that admit AxLEN 0 deny AxLEN `0xFF`, `0xAA` and `0x55` -- whose union walks `AxLEN[7:0]` both ways -- and deny a four-beat FIXED, INCR and WRAP transfer alike. The denial is DECERR because the chapter steers a blocked transaction to an AXI error subordinate and `hw/sys/smc/doc/fabric.adoc` makes unmatched inbound traffic blocked; the admitted single beat at the same address in the same configuration is the live control.
* Every step reconciles its cell count against the AR and AW handshakes the port accepted, counted on the `ext_in_*` pins rather than in the master.
* AxATOP is not driven. `tb/tb_wrapper_top.sv` ties `smu_axi_in_req.aw.atop` to `'0` and the package carries no ATOP driver, so this leaf makes no ATOP claim, as `smu_axi_atomic_operation_test` records for the same reason.
* Shrinking BASE_CONFIG.REGION_SIZE so the local aperture ends at the adopter external window puts that window outside the chiplet, and a JTAG2AXI write to it crosses `smu_axi_out`. The outbound write count moves by exactly one, the boundary carries the address the SMC was given with `w.last` and an OKAY `b.resp`, and the bench responder's memory holds the word.
* Regrowing REGION_SIZE to its reset value is the reprogram state of the SMC aperture map (`smu_xbar_fcov` `c_map_smc_size_reprogrammed`). A zero REGION_SIZE is not driven: `smc_base_config` requires a non-zero power of two, and zero leaves no reachable local aperture, so the closed aperture would route even BASE_CONFIG writes out on `smu_axi_out` and reopen only by reset. The zero-window point is in the <<smu-fcov-phase2,Phase 2 set>> of the coverage plan.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up the primary TAP, route the SMC local aperture, and program inbound entries 0 and 1 over the probe page with `allow_burst = 1`, one matching secure and one matching non-secure traffic.
. Read VERSION_LO and write and read back SCRATCH_COLD under all eight AxPROT encodings, then under AxREGION, AxQOS and AxCACHE at `0x0` and `0xF` and AxLOCK at `0` and `1`, then read VERSION_LO at AxSIZE `0`..`3`.
. Reprogram entries 0 and 1 as single-beat granules at VERSION_LO and SCRATCH_COLD with `allow_burst = 0`, and require DECERR for AxLEN `0xFF`, `0xAA`, `0x55` and for four-beat FIXED, INCR and WRAP on both channels, with the AxLEN 0 transfer at the same address still admitted.
. Disable both entries, shrink BASE_CONFIG.REGION_SIZE to end the local aperture at the external window, and check the shrunk value reaches `smc_region_size_o`.
. Write one word over JTAG2AXI to the external window base, wait for the outbound write count to move, and compare the boundary address, `w.last`, `b.resp` and the responder's memory against the contract.
. Write REGION_SIZE back to its reset value and check `smc_region_size_o` and the JTAG2AXI readback carry it.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-AXIIN-ATTR-PROT` |all eight AxPROT encodings are admitted on AR and AW when one entry matches each `prot[1]` polarity, the reads returning the VERSION_LO RDL reset value and the writes landing in SCRATCH_COLD, over 16 AR and 8 AW address phases the port accepted
|`CHK-AXIIN-ATTR-QUAL` |AxREGION, AxQOS and AxCACHE at `0x0` and `0xF` and AxLOCK at `0` and `1` leave the response and the data unchanged, on AR and AW, over 16 AR and 8 AW address phases
|`CHK-AXIIN-ATTR-SIZE` |AxSIZE `0`..`3` each return the byte slice of the VERSION_LO RDL reset value the size selects
|`CHK-AXIIN-ATTR-LEN` |with `allow_burst = 0` the AxLEN `0xFF`, `0xAA` and `0x55` transfers are refused with DECERR on both channels while AxLEN 0 at the same address is admitted and carries the VERSION_LO reset value
|`CHK-AXIIN-ATTR-BURST` |the same entries refuse a four-beat FIXED, INCR and WRAP transfer with DECERR on both channels
|`CHK-AXIOUT-WRITE` |one SMC write above the shrunk local aperture moves the outbound write count by exactly one, raises `aw_valid`, `w_valid`, `w.last` and `b_valid` on `smu_axi_out`, returns OKAY on `b.resp` at the address the SMC was given, and leaves the written word in the bench responder
|`CHK-SMCMAP-SIZE` |REGION_SIZE regrown to its reset value reaches `smc_region_size_o` within 64 `clk_smu` cycles and reads back over JTAG2AXI
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `fabric`, `hosted`.

'''''


[[sec-108-smu_dtp_sep_dm_dmi_test]]
===== 108 `smu_dtp_sep_dm_dmi_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_dtp_sep_dm_dmi_test`

*BFM / Model*: `ocah_jtag_vip`

*Objective*: reach the SEP debug module from the product JTAG pins: select the SEP STAP, load the SEP TAP's `dmi` register through the spliced scan network, and read `dmstatus`. The path fails if `dmi_core_enable` is 0.

*Test Coverage*:

* S0: the lifecycle posture leaves SEP debug open. The entry names the TEST_DEV shadow image; the exported `lc_state` must leave its pre-sense value and `lcc_dbg_disable_o` must read 0, the posture the lifecycle table gives TEST_DEV. The DTP gates the SEP STAP host interface with `dbg_disable_i.stap_sep` (integrator guide, STAP Scan Chain Topology), so under the default image, which carries no valid LC_STATE word, the SEP STAP cannot be selected at all.
* S1: PTAP IDCODE is the configured IEEE packing.
* S2: TAP_3DCR selects the SEP STAP on the SEP=1 chain (`io`, `smc`, `sep`, `extra0`); host TMS then follows the PTAP, observed on the bench taps `tb_stap_sep_tck` / `tb_stap_sep_tms`. An unselected SEP STAP parks TMS. With the STAP spliced, the PTAP IDCODE reads back at the depth the network layout gives it.
* S3: while the PTAP 3DCR select is set, the STAP chain replaces the TDR mux output on every IR and DR scan (`jtag_ptap` architecture, "STAP selection"), and a selected STAP splices its host TAP's register TDI-side of its own SIB, so a scan is the PTAP register, the SIBs of the STAPs ahead of the SEP, the SEP TAP's register, the SEP's SIB, then the SIBs behind it (DTP scan reference model `chain_layout`). The IR scan loads `TAP_3DCR` in the PTAP and `dmi` (`5'h11`) in the SEP TAP. A `dmcontrol` write sets `dmactive` first, since the RISC-V Debug Specification holds the module in its reset state until it is 1; a `dmi` read of `dmstatus` (op=1) followed by a nop scan then returns status 0 and `version=2`, the RISC-V Debug Specification 0.13 encoding the VeeR EL2 core implements. That read is the `dmi_mux` core aperture and is gated by `dmi_core_enable`.
* Existing SEP STAP IDCODE / TRST / RSTVEC leaves do not enter `dmi_mux` and are not this card.
* Not claimed: abstract commands, SBA, DTMCS (`IR=5'h10` stays in the DTM).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Wait for the lifecycle posture to settle and require `lc_state` and `lcc_dbg_disable_o` to match the TEST_DEV posture.
. Drive `smu_dtp_sep_dm_dmi_test_seq` over the wrapper's product JTAG.
. Select the SEP STAP, compose every following scan over the spliced network, load `dmi` into the SEP TAP, set `dmcontrol.dmactive`, and read `dmstatus`.
. Emit the evidence tokens listed under Checkers.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-DMI-JTAG-READY` |PTAP IDCODE matches the configured IEEE packing
|`CHK-SEP-DMI-STAP-SEL` |selected SEP STAP host TMS (bench tap `tb_stap_sep_tms`) shows the same edge count as the PTAP TMS, and more than while unselected
|`CHK-SEP-DMI-DMSTATUS` |the `dmi` read of `dmstatus` completes with status 0 and `version=2` (Debug Specification 0.13)
|assertion |sep_dmi incomplete s1=<value> s2=<value> s3=<value>
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the scoreboard reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `dtp_under_smu`, `hosted`.

'''''
[[sec-109-smu_sep_lcc_state_rma_sip_0_test]]
===== 109 `smu_sep_lcc_state_rma_sip_0_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_lcc_state_rma_sip_0_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; one image of the shared body `smu_sep_lcc_state_matrix_test.py`)

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_lcc_flow` driving the DEMOTE registers; eFuse shadow preload `hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/sep_efuse_shadow_lc_rma_sip_0.preload` supplied by the testbench

*Objective*: Lifecycle decode for this state: `lc_state` must reach `0xD2` at the SMC, with `dbg_disable` clear at the DTP.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smu_sep_lcc_flow_test` proves the firmware-driven demote reaches every consumer, but it runs in one lifecycle state, so it cannot tell a real decode from a wire. This family runs the same firmware against different eFuse images.
* The eFuse image is the one LCC input firmware cannot drive -- it is sampled before the CPU runs -- so it comes from the testbench side; everything downstream of it is still driven by the firmware.
* Expectations arrive as plusargs rather than being derived in the sequence, so each testlist entry states the contract for its own image: `+lcc_expect_lc_state`, `+lcc_expect_demote_effective`, `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled`.
* Per Table 50 the demotes gate `feat_ctrl[15:0]` and `feat_ctrl[31:16]` in TEST_DEV and PROD but have no entry in PROD_END. The check that can fail is a delta against the reset baseline.
* RMA_SiP's `feat_ctrl` is the complement of the image's `sip_dis`, all-ones with this image, so like TEST_DEV it cannot show a closed-to-open demote; a post-run open low-32 does not prove the write did anything here. The `lc_state` decode is the load-bearing half.
* This entry's contract: `+lcc_expect_lc_state=0xD2`, `+lcc_expect_demote_effective=1`, `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled=0`.
* Runs in the `sep_rtl_no_shadow` mode so the entry's own preload -- not the default shadow image -- is the one sampled.
* The boundary outputs the compares read -- `smc_lc_state_in_o`, `lcc_dbg_disable_o`, `lcc_feat_ctrl_o`, `lcc_demote_state_{1,2}_o` -- are sampled X-strict: an X or Z sample fails the leaf instead of reading as the passing value.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Preload the SEP eFuse shadow registers from this entry's image, before the CPU runs.
. Sample the reset baseline of `feat_ctrl[31:0]`, `lc_state` and `dbg_disable`.
. Run the same `sep_smu_lcc_flow` firmware and require it to reach its pass loop.
. Compare `lc_state` against `0xD2`, the post-run `feat_ctrl[31:0]` state against `+lcc_expect_demote_effective=1`, and `dbg_disable` against `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled=0`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-STATE-DECODE` |this eFuse image produced SMC `lc_state` `0xD2` as contracted -- the LCC is decoding the image, not passing a fixed value
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-STATE-PROFILE` |`feat_ctrl[31:0]` and `dbg_disable` `0` match Table 50 for this state, with the demote asserted at the boundary either way
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_lifecycle`.

'''''
[[sec-110-smu_sep_lcc_state_rma_sip_1_test]]
===== 110 `smu_sep_lcc_state_rma_sip_1_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_lcc_state_rma_sip_1_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; one image of the shared body `smu_sep_lcc_state_matrix_test.py`)

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_lcc_flow` driving the DEMOTE registers; eFuse shadow preload `hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/sep_efuse_shadow_lc_rma_sip_1.preload` supplied by the testbench

*Objective*: Lifecycle decode for this state: `lc_state` must reach `0xC3` at the SMC, with `dbg_disable` clear at the DTP.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smu_sep_lcc_flow_test` proves the firmware-driven demote reaches every consumer, but it runs in one lifecycle state, so it cannot tell a real decode from a wire. This family runs the same firmware against different eFuse images.
* The eFuse image is the one LCC input firmware cannot drive -- it is sampled before the CPU runs -- so it comes from the testbench side; everything downstream of it is still driven by the firmware.
* Expectations arrive as plusargs rather than being derived in the sequence, so each testlist entry states the contract for its own image: `+lcc_expect_lc_state`, `+lcc_expect_demote_effective`, `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled`.
* Per Table 50 the demotes gate `feat_ctrl[15:0]` and `feat_ctrl[31:16]` in TEST_DEV and PROD but have no entry in PROD_END. The check that can fail is a delta against the reset baseline.
* RMA_SiP's `feat_ctrl` is the complement of the image's `sip_dis`, all-ones with this image, so like TEST_DEV it cannot show a closed-to-open demote; a post-run open low-32 does not prove the write did anything here. The `lc_state` decode is the load-bearing half.
* This entry's contract: `+lcc_expect_lc_state=0xC3`, `+lcc_expect_demote_effective=1`, `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled=0`.
* Runs in the `sep_rtl_no_shadow` mode so the entry's own preload -- not the default shadow image -- is the one sampled.
* The boundary outputs the compares read -- `smc_lc_state_in_o`, `lcc_dbg_disable_o`, `lcc_feat_ctrl_o`, `lcc_demote_state_{1,2}_o` -- are sampled X-strict: an X or Z sample fails the leaf instead of reading as the passing value.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Preload the SEP eFuse shadow registers from this entry's image, before the CPU runs.
. Sample the reset baseline of `feat_ctrl[31:0]`, `lc_state` and `dbg_disable`.
. Run the same `sep_smu_lcc_flow` firmware and require it to reach its pass loop.
. Compare `lc_state` against `0xC3`, the post-run `feat_ctrl[31:0]` state against `+lcc_expect_demote_effective=1`, and `dbg_disable` against `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled=0`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-STATE-DECODE` |this eFuse image produced SMC `lc_state` `0xC3` as contracted -- the LCC is decoding the image, not passing a fixed value
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-STATE-PROFILE` |`feat_ctrl[31:0]` and `dbg_disable` `0` match Table 50 for this state, with the demote asserted at the boundary either way
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_lifecycle`.

'''''
[[sec-111-smu_sep_lcc_state_rma_chip_1_test]]
===== 111 `smu_sep_lcc_state_rma_chip_1_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_sep_lcc_state_rma_chip_1_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`; one image of the shared body `smu_sep_lcc_state_matrix_test.py`)

*BFM / Model*: the SEP DV image `hw/sys/sep/dv/fw/tests/sep_smu_lcc_flow` driving the DEMOTE registers; eFuse shadow preload `hw/sys/smu/dv/assets/sep_efuse_shadow_lc_rma_chip_1.preload` supplied by the testbench

*Objective*: Lifecycle decode for this state: `lc_state` must reach `0x87` at the SMC, with `dbg_disable` clear at the DTP.

*Test Coverage*:

* `smu_sep_lcc_flow_test` proves the firmware-driven demote reaches every consumer, but it runs in one lifecycle state, so it cannot tell a real decode from a wire. This family runs the same firmware against different eFuse images.
* The eFuse image is the one LCC input firmware cannot drive -- it is sampled before the CPU runs -- so it comes from the testbench side; everything downstream of it is still driven by the firmware.
* Expectations arrive as plusargs rather than being derived in the sequence, so each testlist entry states the contract for its own image: `+lcc_expect_lc_state`, `+lcc_expect_demote_effective`, `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled`.
* Per Table 50 the demotes gate `feat_ctrl[15:0]` and `feat_ctrl[31:16]` in TEST_DEV and PROD but have no entry in PROD_END. The check that can fail is a delta against the reset baseline.
* RMA_CHIP_1 is the second RMA_CHIPLET code (`4'b011x`) and its `feat_ctrl` is all-ones by state, so like TEST_DEV it cannot show a closed-to-open demote; a post-run open low-32 does not prove the write did anything here. The `lc_state` decode is the load-bearing half.
* This entry's contract: `+lcc_expect_lc_state=0x87`, `+lcc_expect_demote_effective=1`, `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled=0`.
* Runs in the `sep_rtl_no_shadow` mode so the entry's own preload -- not the default shadow image -- is the one sampled.
* The boundary outputs the compares read -- `smc_lc_state_in_o`, `lcc_dbg_disable_o`, `lcc_feat_ctrl_o`, `lcc_demote_state_{1,2}_o` -- are sampled X-strict: an X or Z sample fails the leaf instead of reading as the passing value.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Preload the SEP eFuse shadow registers from this entry's image, before the CPU runs.
. Sample the reset baseline of `feat_ctrl[31:0]`, `lc_state` and `dbg_disable`.
. Run the same `sep_smu_lcc_flow` firmware and require it to reach its pass loop.
. Compare `lc_state` against `0x87`, the post-run `feat_ctrl[31:0]` state against `+lcc_expect_demote_effective=1`, and `dbg_disable` against `+lcc_expect_dbg_disabled=0`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,2",options="header",]
|===
|Checker |Description
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-STATE-DECODE` |this eFuse image produced SMC `lc_state` `0x87` as contracted -- the LCC is decoding the image, not passing a fixed value
|`CHK-SEP-LCC-STATE-PROFILE` |`feat_ctrl[31:0]` and `dbg_disable` `0` match Table 50 for this state, with the demote asserted at the boundary either way
|===

*Pass Criteria*: every checker above holds and the run reports zero errors. Enrolled in: `all`, `sep_lifecycle`.

'''''

[[sec-112-smu_aperture_map_walk_test]]
===== 112 `smu_aperture_map_walk_test`

*Source Module*: `smu_aperture_map_walk_test` (`cocotb_wrapper/tests/`)
